- **myidsan,core**: Phase 4 of the myidsan productization plan ("Operability"): age-based retention for the append-only security audit log, plus a fix -- found by a live bench against a real Keycloak 26 realm -- that closes an auditing gap in federated (OIDC/social) login. A new audit config block (off by default) adds the one sanctioned exception to the trail's otherwise total lack of a delete path: maxRetentionDays (default 365, hard 30-day floor -- a lower configured value is raised and a startup warning logged), frequencyHours (default 24), and archiveDir (default audit-archive). A new IAuditService.PurgeOlderThan archives every row past the cutoff to a JSON-lines file, fsyncs and renames it into place, and only then deletes those rows from the table -- a run that cannot finish its archive deletes nothing -- then records the purge back into the trail it just trimmed (action audit.retention_purge, naming the cutoff, row count, and archive file) so a reader who finds history starting abruptly can tell a deliberate trim from an empty past. Wired as a new periodic scheduler task (myidsan-audit-retention), a no-op unless enabled. Separately, the bench found that providerCallback (the OIDC/Google/GitHub callback handler) audited only successful federated sign-ins: every refused SSO attempt -- a tampered state, a callback missing its flow cookies, an IdP-reported unverified email, a session-issue failure -- was invisible on the Audit log page while a refused password login was recorded. All four rejection paths now record a login.failure entry naming the provider and, where known, the account; the identity-provider unverified-email error also now names the address so the audit record identifies which account was refused. Federated failures deliberately do not advance the per-IP/per-account failed-login lockout, since the credential was checked at the IdP, not guessed against myidsan -- a misconfigured provider (clock skew, rotated secret) must not be able to lock real users out. A live bench against a real Samba AD DC found the same auditing gap in the Kerberos handler: a REJECTED SPNEGO ticket only incremented a Prometheus counter and left no audit record, while a rejected LDAP password login was recorded, so a forged or replayed ticket was invisible on the Audit log page. Refused tickets and unadmitted principals now write a login.failure entry carrying the reason and, where the ticket verified, the principal; a request carrying NO token still does not, because that is the first half of every SPNEGO handshake and auditing it would add one entry per browser request and bury the real rejections. Kerberos failures likewise do not advance the failed-login lockout: a ticket is verified against the keytab rather than guessed, and a keytab gone stale after a machine-account password rotation would otherwise lock out every domain user at once.
- **core,infra,mymatasan,myseliasan,myidsan,myiotsan**: Two shared-infra hardening items from the myidsan productization plan's Phase 4 (Operability), both suite-wide since they live in infra/db and infra/apphost, not any one app. First, a connection-pool budget for the Postgres/MariaDB client/server DB engines: a new db.pool config block (maxOpenConns, maxIdleConns, maxLifetimeSeconds, maxIdleTimeSeconds, plus an explicit unlimited escape hatch) is applied via dbsql.ApplyPool right after sql.Open, before Ping, in both engines' NewDbCrud. The point is the default: Go's database/sql defaults to unlimited open connections, so a traffic burst could previously exhaust a shared database server's entire connection budget and take down every other application on it, not just this one; an absent/zero pool config now yields a bounded pool (25 open / 5 idle / 30-minute lifetime) instead. ApplyPool also clamps maxIdleConns down to maxOpenConns when misconfigured (database/sql would otherwise silently reduce it while the config kept claiming an unhonoured number), and always sets a lifetime bound even in the unlimited case. SQLite is unaffected -- it stays pinned to a single connection, a correctness constraint (concurrent writers to one file deadlock), not a tunable. Second, a startup statement of the HA/shared-state boundary: infra/apphost now logs, once per boot (warnSharedStateBoundary), whether the session cache is shared (redis -- confirms the app can run behind a load balancer) or per-process (states plainly that the instance is single-instance only, names the consequence -- users signed out on every load-balancer switch, all sessions lost on restart -- and the fix). A distributed transaction lock configured alongside a per-process cache logs a WARNING, since a distributed lock only makes sense with more than one instance and pairing it with a per-process session cache is a self-inconsistent deployment. docs/MYIDSAN_PRODUCTIZATION_PLAN.md sections 4.2 and 4.3 are updated to reflect this (4.3's connection-pool bullet is done; its log-file-size-cap bullet remains outstanding, and a prior inaccurate claim there -- that log cleanup only prunes DB rows, not files on disk -- is corrected).

### Changed

- **myidsan,docs**: Closes §4.6 of docs/MYIDSAN_PRODUCTIZATION_PLAN.md ("Reverse-proxy guidance") with a documentation-only delivery -- no Go code changed. New deploy/reverse-proxy/ holds a working nginx-myidsan.conf (HTTP->HTTPS 308 redirect, TLS 1.2/1.3, HSTS, X-Frame-Options, no-cache on auth responses, and commented-out directives to disable upstream keep-alive for Kerberos SPNEGO) and an equivalent Caddyfile.myidsan, plus a README explaining the app-side half (enableTLS:false, nonTlsPorts, bind to loopback); why the public URL must not drift (registered redirect URIs match exactly, and a drifted URL fails every relying app at the callback, after the user already signed in); that X-Forwarded-* is a claim from the client, not a fact, so the proxy must overwrite rather than append it; setting rateLimit.trustedProxies so the audit trail and failed-login lockout key on the real client instead of the whole building; a warning not to set Caddy's trusted_proxies at the outermost edge (that makes Caddy start trusting inbound X-Forwarded-*, the opposite of an internet-facing hop); the Kerberos connection-affinity requirement (SPNEGO authenticates a connection, not a request); an air-gapped-TLS note; and a verification checklist. The plan also documents, with an explicit severity assessment, the known gap where middlewares.IsSecureRequest trusts X-Forwarded-Proto:https from any caller unlike ClientIP's trustedProxies allow-list: because the check is r.TLS!=nil || XFP==https, the header can only make a request look MORE secure, never less, so a genuine HTTPS request cannot be downgraded -- it only affects cookie-name/Secure-flag selection, making the exposure a self-inflicted denial of service (a caller asserting the header over cleartext gets Secure cookies its own browser then refuses to store), not privilege escalation or session fixation. The fix is deliberately deferred (five call sites across shared middleware, myidsan and myseliasan; it decides cookie naming suite-wide) and mitigated meanwhile by having the sample configs strip the header at the edge. deploy/README-myidsan.md, deploy/README.md (mymatasan), deploy/README-myseliasan.md, deploy/README-myiotsan.md, docs/TECHNICAL_SPEC.md, docs/HOWTO.md's Kerberos SPNEGO section, and the root README.md now cross-reference deploy/reverse-proxy/ from their existing TLS/rate-limit/trustedProxies mentions, since the X-Forwarded-*/trustedProxies trust model applies to all four apps even though the sample configs themselves are myidsan-specific.
## 2026-07-29 — myidsan 1.33.0, core 1.67.0 (6594a8d)

### Security

- **myidsan,core**: Phase 3 of the myidsan productization plan ("Policy"): configurable password strength, optional MFA enforcement, bcrypt-hashed relying-app client secrets, per-account login lockout, and CSRF protection on the server-rendered auth pages. A new passwordPolicy config block (minLength default 12, optional upper/lower/digit/symbol requirements defaulting off, an embedded common-password denylist defaulting on, and a same-as-username rejection) is now enforced at all four paths that ever set a human-chosen password -- self-registration, admin-provisioned account creation (POST /api/user-credential, previously the only path with no strength check at all), authenticated change-password, and the self-service token-based reset -- via a new ValidatePassword funnel; server-generated credentials (the bootstrap/temporary 16-character CSPRNG passwords) are exempt by design, and GET /api/login/password-policy publishes the effective rules for the UI. A new mfa config block (policy: off/optional/required, default optional; requiredRoleIds to narrow required to specific roles; applyToDirectory, default false, to also cover LDAP-bound accounts) adds a shared AccessPrincipal.MustEnrollMfa flag and MfaEnrollmentRequiredCode sentinel, checked by the RBAC middleware after the password gate: a user who owes a factor still gets a session but is pinned to the enrollment screen, never refused outright, so switching the policy on cannot lock out every existing administrator at once. Relying-app client secrets (app_auth_config) move from an unsalted single-round SHA-256 -- a database read used to hand an attacker every operator-chosen client secret at GPU speed -- to bcrypt; the token endpoint accepts both the current and legacy hash form and rewrites a legacy row to bcrypt the first time its secret is presented, so an existing install migrates itself with no operator action. The shared failed-login lockout now keys on both the connecting IP and the submitted account identifier (previously IP-only), closing a gap where a password spray distributed across many source addresses against one account was completely unthrottled; the lockout check moved to after request-body decoding on the affected handlers so the account key is available, and token-based steps (MFA code redemption, the forgot-password request) stay source-keyed only since they present a challenge token or an unproven identifier rather than a password. Finally, the server-rendered /api/auth/{login,mfa,forgot,reset} pages -- public routes that never pass through the session-bound CSRF middleware and previously had none -- gain a session-less double-submit CSRF token (cookie + hidden field, __Host--prefixed over TLS) validated on every POST.
## 2026-07-29 — myidsan 1.32.0, myseliasan 1.46.0, core 1.66.0 (4ca5706)

### Added

- **myidsan,myseliasan**: myidsan's Apps page can now export a registered app's SSO client settings as a small JSON bundle, and myseliasan's Settings > Single Sign-On section can import that same bundle into its form. The bundle (`{kind:"myidsan.sso.client", version:1, ...}`) carries issuer, audience, provider base URL, client ID, redirect base URL/path, and session TTL, plus the client secret only when one was just generated in that browser tab (myidsan stores only a hash and never returns it otherwise); a permanent note above the Export button states which case applies. On the myseliasan side, Import parses the file client-side, refuses a wrong `kind` or a `version` newer than the build understands, and fills the SSO form fields without saving -- the operator still reviews and presses the existing Save. Fields absent from the bundle (notably a missing secret) are left alone rather than blanked, matching the settings editor's existing 'blank = keep current' semantics. This removes the main source of federated-login misconfiguration: client ID, audience, and redirect URL must match byte-for-byte across the two consoles, and retyping them by hand is exactly where operators slip.
- **myidsan**: Phase 1 of the myidsan productization plan: passphrase-encrypted backup and restore for the whole identity store, closing the gap where the only documented disaster-recovery procedure was "copy the database and secret/atrest.key yourself, as a pair." A new superadmin-only surface (GET /api/backup/sections, POST /api/backup/{export,preview,restore}, not delegatable through the RBAC permission matrix even by grant) produces or consumes a single `.idbackup` file covering six ordered, selectable sections: access (roles/permissions), identity (users/groups/avatars), MFA (TOTP factors/recovery codes), apps (registry/auth config/redirect URIs), federation (directory config/group mappings), and the SSO CA. The design point that makes a restore usable on a different machine: the two at-rest-sealed values (TOTP secrets, the LDAP bind password) are unsealed on export and re-sealed with the destination host's own at-rest key on restore, so the archive is never a dead end (a naive sealed-bytes copy would restore cleanly and then fail every second-factor check) -- the at-rest key itself never travels. Restore remaps every foreign key onto freshly-created destination ids and skips any row whose parent wasn't restored rather than leaving a dangling or reinterpreted grant, drops every live session afterward (a pre-restore session would still carry pre-restore authority), and marks first-run setup complete. Reachable from a new Backup & restore page in the nav and from a new "restore from a backup instead" path on the first-run wizard's welcome step. Verified end-to-end against a live instance: enrol MFA, export, destroy the database and the at-rest key, fresh-install, restore, sign in with the pre-disaster password, redeem a TOTP code generated from the pre-disaster secret.
- **myidsan,core**: Phase 2 of the myidsan productization plan ("Accountability"): an append-only security audit log, session administration, and step-up re-authentication. A new AuditLog entity/service records login success/failure/lockout/logout (with the sign-in method), MFA enroll/disable/admin-reset, user create/update/delete, directory config updates, password-reset resolution, backup export/restore, session revocation, and step-up success/failure -- the service exposes only Record and List, with no update or delete path at all, so the trail cannot be edited from inside the product. A failed sign-in is recorded with no actor id and the attempted (possibly bogus) identifier as the actor label, since the caller has no account yet; every event captures the User-Agent, which myseliasan's equivalent audit log does not. A new superadmin-only Audit log page (GET /api/audit, filterable by action/outcome/actor/target/date range) and GET /api/audit/export.csv share one filter parser so an export always matches what the screen showed; the CSV defuses spreadsheet formula injection on every attacker-influenced column (a failed-login username, a User-Agent) by quoting a leading =/+/-/@/tab/CR. Client addresses recorded anywhere in the trail go through a newly-exported domain/utils/middlewares.ClientIP/ParseTrustedProxies, extracted from the rate limiter's previously-private copy so the two features cannot disagree on which peers may declare a forwarded address -- unlike myseliasan's audit log, which trusts X-Forwarded-For unconditionally. Separately, the long-declared-but-never-written domain/entities.UserSession table is now actually populated (gained IpAddress/UserAgent/LastSeenAt) by a new ISessionService: the cache entry at sso:session:<sid> remains the authority the auth middleware checks, this table is only an index so sessions can be listed per user, and a revoke deletes the cache entry first so a bookkeeping failure can never leave a live session claiming to be dead. A self-service Sessions panel on the Profile page (GET/DELETE /api/session, POST /api/session/revoke-all, deliberately auth-only and not RBAC-matrix-gated -- the person whose laptop was stolen needs this screen unconditionally) lets a user list and end their own sessions or sign out everywhere else; a superadmin can end another account's sessions from the Users page (/api/session-admin). Disabling or deleting an account now ends its sessions too, closing a real gap where a disabled account's already-issued session kept validating against auth-only routes (/api/profile/*, /api/mfa) for up to its full 72-hour lifetime, since the cached session carries no account-status flag. Finally, a new 5-minute cache-backed step-up re-authentication marker (password, plus TOTP when a factor is enrolled) now gates the four most dangerous things a stolen 72-hour superadmin cookie could otherwise do with no further proof of identity: backup export, backup restore, resetting another user's MFA, and resolving a password-reset request. A failed step-up attempt is itself audited.

### Changed

- **myidsan**: The myidsan Apps page's guided app-registration form is compacted on UX feedback that it carried too much text to actually be read. No guidance was removed -- it is now disclosed on demand instead of all at once. Every per-field explanation, section lede, preview-panel note and checklist step detail moved behind a new (i) info tip beside its label, which opens on click and closes on Escape, an outside click, or a second click. Each field's worked example is no longer a separate line under the input; it is the input's own placeholder, so the shape of a valid value (Fleet Console, fleet-console, https://fleet.example.com, the TTL numbers) is visible without any prose. The redirect-URI matching rules and the sign-in troubleshooting list became collapsed-by-default callouts, and the "How app registration works" primer now auto-opens only on a genuinely first registration (no apps registered yet) rather than on every new app. Validation messages deliberately stay permanently visible, since those have to be seen rather than asked for. Measured on a live instance, the full editor's rendered height dropped from 3382px to 2337px, about 31% shorter. Frontend-only: no API, schema, or config changes.

### Security

- **core,myidsan**: Phase 0 of the myidsan productization plan: a batch of truth-and-hygiene fixes across shared infra and myidsan. The dead RequirePKCE/AllowRefreshToken/RefreshTokenTTLSeconds controls are removed from the Apps form and admin API (nothing in the authorize/token flow ever read them; the entity columns stay, reserved, for the future OIDC-conformance phase). The shared `loginSecurity` config block is now read only through a resolver that treats an absent block as ON with tuned defaults -- previously an absent block silently decoded to Enabled=false, which is what shipped in deploy/dist/myseliasan-config.json and the myidsan/myseliasan dev configs, making the failed-login lockout a silent no-op on those installs. `sso.internalToken` (guards /api/sso/introspect) is now refused at boot if it is a known placeholder value (it shipped as the literal `change-me-in-production` in myidsan's config files and had no such guard before); a placeholder is dropped rather than carried forward, disabling the endpoint instead of leaving a publicly-known token protecting it. Eight committed TLS private keypairs (all four apps) are untracked and apps/*/certs/ is now gitignored; real secrets (jwt secret, admin123, an internal-token placeholder, a live Google OAuth client_id) are scrubbed from apps/myidsan/config.json/config.dev.json. Swagger UI is now fully self-hosted (vendored swagger-ui-dist, served via go:embed) instead of loading from cdn.jsdelivr.net, which previously broke /swagger on air-gapped installs and violated the apps' own script-src 'self' CSP even when the CDN was reachable. Correctness/security fixes: myidsan's default-login path no longer discloses account state (disabled, federated-only) before a password has been verified -- an unknown username and a federated-only account both pay the cost of a dummy bcrypt compare so the timing and error are indistinguishable from a wrong password, and legacy plaintext password/SSO-internal-token comparisons are now constant-time; the federated-login continue-path sanitizer now rejects a backslash-prefixed open-redirect bypass (`/\evil.example`) that browsers normalise into a navigable host; toast `kind:'danger'` (matched no CSS class) is corrected to `'error'` at all 7 call sites; bulk-delete on Groups/Roles/Endpoints now asks for confirmation. A new CI workflow (`.github/workflows/go-check.yml`) runs build/vet/test on every push/PR plus an advisory govulncheck, and caught a stale test that had been failing unnoticed (fixed in infra/apphost/run_test.go).

## 2026-07-28 — myidsan 1.25.0 (91932cf)

### Changed

- **myidsan**: The myidsan Apps page's app-registration form is redesigned from a bare CRUD sheet into a guided walkthrough, since one wrong field here (code, audience, redirect URI) fails later at runtime on the relying app's side with a terse error far from this screen. A data-derived step rail (register -> SSO client -> redirect URIs -> connect) tracks progress against what actually exists rather than a wizard cursor. A collapsible "How app registration works" primer (open by default for a new app) explains the authorize -> login -> one-time code -> token-exchange flow. Every registry and SSO-client field now carries inline guidance and a worked example, plus client-side validation that blocks Save: the app code is held to a slug shape, code/audience are checked against the already-loaded app list for a ukey1/ukey2 clash before it can hit the database as a 500, and the base URL is parsed with a (non-blocking) warning for plain http on a non-localhost host. A live "Configure the app with these" panel renders the provider base URL, audience, redirect URI, authorize URL, token URL, and a ready-to-paste sso config.json block, each with a Copy button. A "Connect the app" section adds a handoff checklist and a troubleshooting table mapping the literal server errors (client is not registered / redirect_uri is not registered / audience not registered for client / client secret not valid) to their likely cause. The SSO client form's Require PKCE and Allow refresh tokens hints now say explicitly that both are recorded on the client row but not yet enforced -- the token endpoint only implements the authorization_code grant. Frontend-only: no API, schema, or config changes.
## 2026-07-28 — myidsan 1.24.0 (4c760e8)

### Added

- **myidsan**: myidsan gains a self-service TOTP second factor (RFC 6238, pure-Go infra/mfa package + a new github.com/skip2/go-qrcode dependency for on-box QR rendering -- no CDN, air-gap safe) for the credentials it verifies itself: local password and LDAP/AD. A self-service Profile page (opened from the account chip) lets any signed-in user enroll (scan a QR or enter the base32 secret manually), confirm, view status, regenerate 10 single-use bcrypt-hashed recovery codes, and disable their own factor (GET/POST/DELETE /api/mfa/*). Once a factor is confirmed, both password login surfaces -- the SPA's POST /api/login/default and /api/login/ldap, and the server-rendered /api/auth/login page -- withhold the session cookie after a correct password and instead return an opaque, single-use, 5-minute, client-fingerprint-bound challenge token; the session is only issued after the token plus a TOTP or recovery code is redeemed at POST /api/login/mfa (SPA) or POST /api/auth/mfa (server-rendered). Kerberos SPNEGO and OIDC/Google/GitHub logins are deliberately not gated -- the upstream IdP owns factor policy for those. The shared secret is sealed at rest the same way the LDAP bind password already is. A superadmin can clear another user's factor at DELETE /api/mfa-admin/{id}; a new RESET_MFA marker file in the data dir (mirroring the existing RESET_ADMIN password-recovery marker) clears the stock superadmin's own factor on next boot for the sole-superadmin lost-device lockout case. A new myidsan_mfa_challenge_total{result} metric tracks issued/success/failed/expired outcomes so a spike in failures flags online guessing against a known password. Separately, myidsan is now listed as an available, downloadable product on the r450k marketing/download site (tag namespace myidsan-v, image ghcr.io/mysayasan/myidsan), joining mymatasan/myiotsan/myseliasan -- its release pipeline already existed but was not yet surfaced to visitors.
- **myidsan**: myidsan gains a self-service "forgot password" account-recovery flow with two channels. A "Forgot your password?" link on both login surfaces posts to a public, rate-limited, always-generic endpoint (POST /api/login/forgot on the SPA, POST /api/auth/forgot on the server-rendered login page) that never reveals whether the submitted identifier matched an account. Channel 1, always on and air-gap safe: a new superadmin "Reset requests" queue (GET/POST /api/password-reset/*) lets an operator resolve a pending request by issuing a one-time, must-change temporary password to hand over out-of-band, or dismiss a bogus one. Channel 2, optional: a new pure-stdlib infra/mailer SMTP sender (no external dependency) can email a single-use, 30-minute self-service reset link (GET/POST /api/auth/reset) when the new smtp config block is enabled -- the sender refuses to authenticate over a non-STARTTLS connection and strips CR/LF from headers to prevent injection. The self-service link deliberately never issues a session, so any enrolled MFA factor is still presented at the user's next normal login. Only LOCAL accounts are eligible; LDAP/Kerberos/OIDC accounts are silently excluded and directed to their upstream identity provider. Live-benched end-to-end: no-oracle response, SMTP self-service reset, admin-queue resolve to temp-password to must-change, single-use token consumption, and dismiss.
- **myidsan**: myidsan's account chip now opens a single self-service "Profile" page consolidating change-password, TOTP MFA management, and a new profile-picture (avatar) editor -- it is a chipOnly route reached only from the account chip in the side rail, never a nav item or dashboard tile, so it doesn't compete for menu space. This ships myidsan's first in-app self-service change-password form (POST /api/login/default/change-password), previously reachable only via the forced first-login screen. Profile pictures are a new UserAvatar entity (one base64-JPEG row per account, never carried in JWT/session claims) and a profileApi surface: self-service GET/POST/DELETE /api/profile/avatar (auth-only, own account) plus a superadmin GET/POST/DELETE /api/profile/avatar/{userId} surface that lets an operator set or clear any user's picture inline from the Users management page (a new per-row avatar + camera button). The client center-crops and resizes the picked image to a 256px JPEG in-browser (canvas) before upload, so the server needs no image codec -- just a MIME allow-list (JPEG/PNG/WebP/GIF) and a 512KB decoded-size cap; a missing avatar is a plain 404 the SPA renders as an initials fallback. Three icons (mail, upload, shield-check) were added to the shared frontend icon set to support the new page. Also fixes a CSS bug where the workspace-header theme dropdown stretched full-width across the content area and could open off-screen; it is now content-sized and opens downward, right-aligned -- a visual bug fix with no behavior or API change.
## 2026-07-26 — myseliasan 1.44.0 (84c1c2c)

### Added

- **myseliasan**: Added an in-app Settings page to the myseliasan control plane, giving it its first self-service config editor (mymatasan already had one). A new Settings nav item under the System group (superadmin-only in both the nav gate and the API) exposes a SAFE SUBSET of config.json for read/edit: localAuth, sso, pairing, security (JWT secret, allowed origins, TLS paths, CSP, rate-limit tiers), storage (file storage/cleanup, cache/Redis), and logging (app/API log retention, Prometheus telemetry) -- db, server, and bootstrap stay file-only since a bad value there could take the app offline. Because the shared apphost reads these infra blocks only once at boot, PUT /api/settings/{section} validates and writes the change into both the live in-memory config and a surgical, order-preserving patch of config.json (untouched blocks keep their exact original bytes), then reports needsRestart; the page shows a persistent restart banner wired to a new POST /api/system/restart (superadmin-gated, relaunches the process via the existing apphost Restarter and polls health until the new process answers). A per-section Restore defaults (POST /api/settings/{section}/reset) reverts to a one-time first-run snapshot captured automatically and stored in the control-plane DB, encrypted via the existing fleet cipher when encryption at rest is enabled. Secret fields (local admin password, SSO client secret, JWT secret, Redis password) are never sent to the browser -- masked plus a "set" flag, and a blank field on save keeps the current value. The feature grew substantially beyond the initial editor: a System tab on the same page mirrors mymatasan's system panel -- running build/version info and live API/liveness/readiness health, refreshable on demand, alongside the restart control; a cache-provider dropdown reveals a Redis card with a live Test connection button (POST /api/settings/cache/test) that pings Redis with the in-progress form values (blank password falls back to the stored one) before anything is saved; every filesystem-path field (TLS cert/key, SSO CA cert, file-storage folder, log file) gets a Browse... button backed by a new whitelisted, read-only server-side file/folder picker (GET /api/settings/fs/browse, ported from mymatasan, confined to the working dir/data dir/home/common install locations); the Redis database number is a dropdown and fields with canonical values (default ports, multicast address, session-TTL presets, /metrics, callback path, localhost:6379) offer one-click autocomplete suggestions; and every field carries a plain-language (i) tooltip. The Save button is disabled until the section's working copy actually differs from what was loaded, with an inline "unsaved changes" indicator while dirty. Every save/reset (section name only, never values) and the /api/settings and /api/system routes themselves are recorded to the existing audit trail and endpoint catalog, matching the pattern used by the recent Reports feature.
## 2026-07-26 — myseliasan 1.43.0 (fdac25f)

### Added

- **myseliasan**: The Fleet Map's digital twin is no longer buildings-only: a Site now carries a Kind (building / outdoor / point), so an operator can add an outdoor area (a park, yard, car park -- one open ground surface, no storey stacking) or a point asset (a junction, pole, gate, barrier -- no floor plan at all, its cameras reached only through the appliance(s) assigned to it) alongside a regular building, each drawn with its own marker silhouette (disc/square/diamond) and glyph palette on the geographic map. The asset wizard (asset_wizard.js, replacing the old building-only building_wizard.js) leads with 'what is being added' before anything else. Node/camera placement is now exclusive: a camera (or a node's own marker) can hold at most one pin fleet-wide -- a second POST to place an already-placed camera is refused with 409 and a message naming the site/area it currently sits in (a new GET /api/placements fleet-wide index backs the floor editor's palette so it can grey out an already-placed camera up front). The floor editor (floor_editor.js) gained a finer snap grid, a multi-select transform gizmo (move/resize/rotate, oriented to a single selected object), copy/cut/paste of geometry (camera markers excluded -- they cannot be duplicated), a Window tool, per-kind tool sets (Gate/Parking for an outdoor area), a raised-floor (Platform) tool with an adjustable rise that stairs can lock onto as their base, richer stairs (step count, own climb height, up/down direction), mirrorable door hinge/swing, on-canvas camera POV drag handles, and a delete-asset button. The 3D view extrudes windows as glazing, raised floors as carved slabs, stairs resting on their platform, and a down flight carving a stairwell opening into the floor above. The read-only 2D building drill-down now renders this same authored geometry (walls/stairs/platforms/etc.) as a vector overlay over the plan image, so a floor with drawn walls no longer looks empty outside the 3D tab. Building navigation from the rail now opens the specific area that was clicked rather than always the first one.
- **myseliasan**: Added a Reports page to the myseliasan control plane that generates four on-demand, printable PDF reports entirely server-side (a new pure-Go domain/report builder over github.com/go-pdf/fpdf -- no headless browser, so it works on an air-gapped install): Fleet Health (node online/offline roster, certificate expiry, an alert summary over a selectable trailing window), Site & Asset Inventory (per-building asset register with a rendered floor plan per floor -- including the authored walls/doors/windows/stairs/parking/raised-floor geometry from the floor editor, plus camera coverage wedges and markers -- not just the flat plan image), Incident Detail (recent alerts with per-event detail and an inline snapshot when carried), and Security & Access (users/roles/permission matrix/audit trail plus a data-protection attestation, gated to superadmins only, both at the API and hidden from the SPA for anyone else). Every report is served as a PDF attachment (GET /api/reports/{fleet-health,inventory,security,incident}.pdf) and every generation is written to the audit trail as a report.* action, since it is a sensitive bulk read of fleet data. The frontend shows the generated PDF in a preview modal (the browser's own PDF viewer) with Print/Download/Close, rather than triggering a blind download.

## 2026-07-24 — myseliasan 1.39.0 (c803f69)

### Changed

- **myseliasan**: Added early, unwired scaffolding for a planned 'map workspace' redesign of the Fleet Map: components/map/geo_map.js (reworked OpenLayers world map), inspector.js (a single contextual right-pane card for whatever is selected, replacing the current scatter of floating popups), asset_browser.js (a left-pane search + status + Building -> Floor -> Camera tree), floor_view.js (an in-workspace floor surface), and styles/map-workspace.css. None of these are imported anywhere yet -- App.js still lazy-loads the shipped map_page.js/fleet_map.js -- so this commit changes no shipped behavior; it is pure dead code preparing for a future three-pane workspace shell intended to eventually supersede the current map page.
- **myseliasan**: The Fleet Map is now buildings-only: nodes never draw their own pin any more, and every node is reached by drilling into the building it belongs to. Standalone/off-site node placement is removed (the old 'place on map' button, node drag-to-place, and the Nodes-layer toggle are gone); the side rail is now building-centric, listing every building (placed and unplaced) with status/camera count and an expandable list of that building's floors/areas, with nodes not yet assigned to a building surfaced in a separate 'Appliances' section to assign via a dropdown. The in-app floor editor (FloorEditor) gained three new authoring tools -- Round (elliptical rooms), Door (wall openings carved in both 2D and 3D with a lintel), and Stairs (rotatable straight flights, extruded as rising steps in 3D) -- plus zoom (toolbar + Ctrl/Cmd+wheel), a draggable/dockable tool palette and properties inspector (float or dock left/right, stacking when both dock the same side), and an Esc that now cancels an in-progress wall run instead of exiting the whole editor and losing unsaved walls. The building editor also gained a 'Remove plan' action (new DELETE /api/floors/{id}/image endpoint, services.ClearFloorImage) that clears an area's plan picture back to the blank canvas it started as, at the floor's existing pixel dimensions, while leaving every wall/stair/door and camera placement untouched -- the inverse of uploading a plan, and the only way to un-upload one short of deleting the whole area.
- **myseliasan**: The 3D floor view (floor_3d.js) no longer invents a four-sided perimeter wall for a floor with no authored wall segments -- it now extrudes exactly what the operator drew, so an area with no outer wall simply shows a bare slab in 3D, matching the 2D plan. Separately, camera markers in the 3D view now gain parity with the 2D plan's unread-notification indicator: a camera with unread alerts shows a severity-coloured count badge (a billboard sprite, always facing the camera and drawn through walls, 99+ capped) and, on the floor beneath it, a two-wave pulse in the alert's severity colour matching the beacon already used on the fleet-map building markers. In the stacked multi-floor view the badge appears on every floor so an alert a storey down stays visible, but only the active/non-dimmed floor pulses, bounding the motion. The unread count/severity is now part of the 3D scene's rebuild signature so a new alert appears or clears immediately without any other input changing. Also hardened 3D scene teardown so it no longer disposes the shared module-level sprite geometry (three.js sprites share one geometry instance; disposing it broke every sprite created after the first).
## 2026-07-23 — myseliasan 1.37.0 (066b6e6)

### Changed

- **myseliasan**: The Fleet Map's separate 'Floor plans' tab is gone; buildings are now created and authored entirely from the geographic map. A new '+ Add building' wizard collects the building's name, glyph, and whether it is a single area or has several areas (floors/wings/rooms), creating the site plus one floor-plan row per area with a server-generated blank canvas (new POST /api/sites/{id}/areas, backed by services.AddBlankFloor) instead of the old client-side rasterise-and-multipart-upload trick. Saving enters map-placing mode; dropping the marker opens a new near-fullscreen building editor dialog with area tabs, a node/camera palette, and a single FloorEditor canvas (Select/Wall/Room/Erase drawing tools) that autosaves walls and placements as the operator works. Existing buildings get an edit entry point from the rail and from the read-only building drill-down. Bundled in the same change: floor plans gained a 3D view — a floor's painted wall/room grid plus real-world scale, wall height and per-floor elevation (FloorPlan.Grid/Scale/WallHeight/Elevation, autosaved via a new PUT /api/floors/{id}/model) is extruded into a three.js scene with floors stackable by elevation, and camera placements gained MountHeight/Pitch (via the existing PUT /api/placements/{id}, now accepting two more optional fields) to stand each camera's coverage as a 3D cone rather than only a flat 2D wedge. The building drill-down (BuildingFloorView) gained a 2D/3D toggle and a 'stack floors' option; three.js is code-split so it only loads when 3D is actually opened.
## 2026-07-23 — myidsan 1.21.0 (a4ce383)

### Added

- **myidsan**: myidsan's federated login gains a provider-registry seam (infra/login.Registry, infra/login.RedirectProvider) so identity providers beyond Google/GitHub (LDAP/AD, Kerberos SPNEGO, OIDC - see docs/MYIDSAN_ENTERPRISE_SSO_PLAN.md) can be added without touching routing again. The four hardcoded Google/GitHub routes are replaced by one generic pair, GET /api/login/{provider} and GET /api/callback/{provider}, driven by the registry that NewLoginApi builds and returns; NewFederatedAuthApi and the SPA's provider buttons now read the same registry instead of hardcoded config checks. GET /api/login/providers gains an authoritative list:[{key,displayName}] field alongside the legacy {google,github} booleans. Account resolution for every federated login now goes through one new services.UpsertFederated, which matches strictly on (provider, subject) rather than email - fixing a real account-takeover risk where the old per-provider Google/GitHub callback code merged federated logins onto any existing account with a matching email, letting a same-email account registered at a different provider silently take it over. A same-email account with no identity bound yet may still claim one, once; a bound conflict is refused (ErrFederatedIdentityConflict); a full miss creates a new pending-clearance account (role 0), matching every other new-user path in the suite. domain/entities.UserLogin gains SsoProvider/SsoSubject columns (auto-migrated by bootstrap).
- **myidsan**: myidsan gains LDAP/Active Directory login (Phase 1 of docs/MYIDSAN_ENTERPRISE_SSO_PLAN.md), covering AD, Samba AD, FreeIPA, OpenLDAP, and 389-ds. A new pure-Go infra/login/ldap.go performs a service-account bind, a single-entry search from a filter-escaped username template (ambiguous or unmatched results fail closed), then binds as the found user with the supplied password; transport is always TLS (implicit ldaps or StartTLS, optional pinned CA) and an empty/NUL password is refused before any network I/O, closing the classic anonymous-bind bypass. The directory connection is a new DB-backed singleton (DirectoryConfig, apps/myidsan/entities/directory_config.go) configured from a new Federation -> Directory admin page (GET/PUT /api/directory-config, POST /api/directory-config/test which validates the submitted-but-unsaved settings and never binds as a sample user); the bind password is encrypted at rest via infra/atrest, myidsan's first at-rest-encrypted secret. A new FederatedGroupMapping table maps a directory group (matched case-insensitively, highest priority wins) to a myidsan role, resolved on every LDAP login; an Authoritative toggle decides whether the mapping re-applies every login or only seeds a still-pending account once - no match leaves an account's role untouched, so pending-clearance stands. POST /api/login/ldap (SPA) and a new account-type selector on the server-rendered /api/auth/login page (rendered only while directory login is enabled) both authenticate through services.IDirectoryService.AuthenticateLdap, which resolves the account via the existing strict (provider, subject) UpsertFederated dedup shared with Google/GitHub. Separately, this change adds a per-IP failed-login lockout (the same shared LoginGuard mymatasan/myiotsan already use, config block loginSecurity) to myidsan's local login, the new LDAP login, and the federated login page's POST - myidsan previously had no failed-login throttling on any credential surface. A new myidsan_federated_login_total{provider,result} metric counts LDAP outcomes (invalid credential, unreachable, ambiguous, no email, identity conflict, disabled, inactive) so directory failures are visible in aggregate rather than only as individual failed sign-ins.
- **myidsan**: myidsan gains Kerberos SPNEGO single sign-on (Phase 2 of docs/MYIDSAN_ENTERPRISE_SSO_PLAN.md): a domain-joined browser's existing Windows/AD login authenticates it against myidsan with no password prompt. A new pure-Go infra/login/kerberos.go (github.com/jcmturner/gokrb5/v8, pinned v8.4.4) loads an exported service keytab at boot and verifies a request's Authorization: Negotiate token via SPNEGO AcceptSecContext; a missing token yields a 401 + WWW-Authenticate: Negotiate challenge (not an error), while a bad/expired ticket or a realm outside the configured allow-list is a real refusal. The verified principal carries no email/groups by itself, so a new infra/login.LdapLookup (service-bind search, no user bind) and apps/myidsan/services/directory.go's ResolveDirectoryUser resolve it through the same directory and group->role mapping a password/LDAP login for that person would use - one person, one account, regardless of credential path; a directory-less install falls back to a principal-derived standalone identity instead. GET /api/login/kerberos is the new endpoint (apps/myidsan/apis/login.go, NewLoginApi refactored to take a LoginApiOptions struct): success issues the session and redirects to the pending continue target; any failure (rejected ticket, disallowed realm, unresolvable principal) redirects to /api/auth/login?error=sso_failed with an inline message rather than a dead-end 401 page, and is counted in myidsan_federated_login_total{provider="kerberos",result=...} (ticket_rejected, realm_refused, unreachable, not_in_directory, identity_conflict, inactive). apps/myidsan/apis/federated_auth.go renders a configurable-label 'Windows (SSO)' button alongside the existing social/LDAP options. A new kerberos config block (enabled, keytabPath, servicePrincipal, onlyRealms, displayLabel) controls it; apps/myidsan/app/app.go loads the keytab at boot and, on a bad/missing keytab, logs a warning and leaves Kerberos 'not offered' rather than failing startup, mirroring how a half-configured OAuth provider is handled.
- **myidsan**: myidsan gains generic OpenID Connect (OIDC) login as a relying party (Phase 3 of docs/MYIDSAN_ENTERPRISE_SSO_PLAN.md), so it can federate against any spec-compliant enterprise IdP (Keycloak, Authentik, ADFS, Entra, ...) without a bespoke integration per provider. A new login.oidc[] config array (infra/login/login_models.go's OidcProviderConfigModel: key, display_name, issuer_url, client_id/secret, redirect_url, scopes, ca_cert_path, groups_claim, insecure_skip_email_verified) drives infra/login/oidc.go (github.com/coreos/go-oidc/v3, a thin layer over the already-vendored golang.org/x/oauth2): NewOidcLogin runs OIDC discovery against the issuer at startup (10s timeout, optional pinned CA for private-CA intranet IdPs) and builds an infra/login.RedirectProvider per configured entry, so GET /api/login/{key} and GET /api/callback/{key} work exactly like Google/GitHub. The login leg runs PKCE (S256) plus a nonce, both riding single-use HttpOnly cookies scoped to the callback path (mirroring the existing state-cookie pattern); the callback verifies the id_token against the discovered JWKS, checks the nonce, and maps claims via the exported/unit-tested OidcIdentityFromClaims: an explicit email_verified=false is refused unless the per-provider override is set (an absent claim is accepted), and groups_claim feeds Identity.Groups from either a JSON array or ADFS's bare-string shape. Accounts bind to "oidc:<key>" (Identity.Provider) - changing an entry's key orphans existing federated accounts, called out in the config doc comment. infra/login/provider.go's BuildRegistry registers each configured entry only when client_id/client_secret are present and discovery succeeds; either failure (typo'd issuer, IdP down at boot, bad CA) logs a WARNING and skips just that provider, the same fail-soft policy Google/GitHub/Kerberos already use - the rest of the login page is unaffected and it comes back on the next restart. "ldap" and "kerberos" were added to the registry's reserved-key set so an operator-chosen OIDC key can never shadow either fixed route. infra/apphost/run.go's applySensitiveConfig now supports per-provider client-secret env overrides, OIDC_<KEY>_CLIENT_SECRET (key upper-cased, non-alphanumerics mapped to underscore, e.g. corp-idp -> OIDC_CORP_IDP_CLIENT_SECRET). apps/myidsan/services/directory.go gains AdmitExternalIdentity: it wraps UpsertFederated with group->role seeding from FederatedGroupMapping rows scoped to the login's exact provider string, always non-authoritative regardless of the directory's own Authoritative toggle - a mapping only seeds a still-pending account's role once, never re-applies on every login, because an external IdP's groups claim is only ever as fresh as the last login's id_token (unlike LDAP, which myidsan can query for live group truth). This works with the directory itself disabled, since mappings are provider-scoped rows, not directory settings. apps/myidsan/apis/login.go's providerCallback now routes through a new admitRedirectIdentity helper that calls AdmitExternalIdentity when a directory service is wired, falling back to plain UpsertFederated otherwise; social logins are unaffected since they carry no groups. The Federation -> Directory group-mapping editor in the SPA gained an editable Provider field (default "ldap", e.g. "oidc:keycloak") and a Provider column in the mappings table, so OIDC group mappings can be entered alongside LDAP ones.
- **myidsan**: myidsan gains the first-run operator experience mymatasan/myseliasan already have (PR A of the wizard+packaging plan; packaging is a later PR B). app/firstrun.go (ported from apps/myseliasan/app/firstrun.go) prints a console banner ("MyIDSan is ready") and writes an INITIAL_ADMIN_LOGIN.txt recovery file (0600, data dir) whenever the bootstrap superadmin is freshly seeded; a RESET_ADMIN one-shot marker file in the data dir is the lock-out recovery path, deleted BEFORE the reset runs so a crash can't re-run it, then force-resets the password/reactivates the account/re-pins the superadmin role via the new services.ResetStockSuperadmin. services/user_login.go's EnsureStockSuperadmin now returns a StockSeedResult{Username,Password,Generated,Seeded} and, when config/env both leave localAuth.password empty, GENERATES a per-install 16-character password (crypto/rand, unambiguous charset) instead of falling back to a hard-coded default - the generated password is never rotated on later boots (only a config/env-supplied one still refreshes in, and only while the account is untouched). A new setup wizard (services/setup_state.go + apis/setup.go, mirroring mymatasan's setup-state contract; persisted in the new shared RuntimeSetting entity under key "setup.state") walks a superadmin through 5 steps - Welcome, register the first relying app (app-registry + auth-config + redirect-uri, secret generated if left blank and shown once), optional LDAP quick-enable, create a personal superadmin account (via the new POST /api/user-credential admin-provisioning endpoint, distinct from self-registration which lands pending), and Done - shown only after the forced password change clears and only until GET /api/setup/state reports completed; every step is skippable and POST /api/setup/complete (or Skip setup) records completion once, shared across browsers.
- **myidsan**: MyIDSan gains full installer/release packaging, mirroring MyMataSan and MySeliaSan: GoReleaser cross-compiled archives (linux/windows/darwin, amd64/arm64), .deb/.rpm packages with a systemd unit and dedicated system user, multi-arch Docker images, and a Windows Inno Setup installer (service registration, generated one-time admin password on the finish page, and a reset-admin-login option). Releases are cut under MyIDSan's own myidsan-v<version> tag namespace via a dedicated release-myidsan workflow.

### Changed

- **myidsan**: MyIDSan's UI adopts the suite's standardized shell and brand mark. The login and auth cards now use the shared BrandLogo (the shield + eye + check mark, tinted violet via --brand-mark) instead of a bespoke glyph on a solid-blue tile that overpowered it. The post-login shell follows MyMataSan/MySeliaSan: a centered BrandLogo in the side rail, an account chip showing the signed-in role with a ghost logout, a pin / auto-hide rail toggle (collapses to an icon strip that slides out on hover), and a slim WorkspaceHeader top strip carrying the language switcher and theme picker.
## 2026-07-23 — myidsan 1.14.0 (85aa058)

### Security

- **myidsan**: GET /api/user-credential (the superadmin-only admin Users listing) was projecting every local account's stored bcrypt password hash into the JSON response, because the read/output UserLoginDto still declared a Userpwd field. Domain-level DTO projection (domain/utils/dtos.Project) copies only fields present on the destination struct, so any struct field on the output DTO is faithfully populated and returned to the client. Removing Userpwd from dtos/output.UserLoginDto stops the hash from ever being copied into a read response, closing an offline-cracking / credential-exposure risk for every superadmin who has loaded the Users admin page.
## 2026-07-21 — myiotsan 0.21.0, mymatasan 1.111.0, myseliasan 1.36.0, core 1.64.0 (db914b1)

### Added

- **myseliasan**: Fleet Map / GIS: a new offline geographic + indoor spatial view of the adopted node fleet, rendered with OpenLayers (vendored into the myseliasan frontend, lazy-loaded so it never weighs down the initial bundle) and a self-hosted Protomaps .pmtiles basemap served by a new Range-served handler (GET /api/basemap/info, GET /api/basemap/tiles.pmtiles) - no tile CDN, so it works on an air-gapped/intranet install like the rest of the app. The Geographic view plots nodes at lat/lon (new ManagedNode.Lat/Lon/MapPlaced fields, set by dragging a pin via PUT /api/nodes/{id}/position) and colors each pin online/warning(cert expiring)/critical(lost)/idle, matching the existing Dashboard cert-health signal. The Floor plans view lets an operator create sites and add a floor plan per site either by uploading an image (PNG/JPEG/GIF, up to 24 MiB) or by drawing one from scratch in a new built-in floor designer (rooms/walls/text/pen tools, grid-snap, undo/redo, multi-select, rotate/flip, pan/zoom) whose vector shapes round-trip through a new FloorPlan.Design field so a drawn plan can be reopened and re-edited; an uploaded photo can likewise be annotated later, with its pristine original preserved as a new FloorPlan.BgPath background layer (GET /api/floors/{id}/background) so re-editing never draws over an already-flattened render (POST /api/floors/{id}/image replaces the rendered image + design). Nodes/cameras are dragged onto a plan as NodePlacement rows keyed to pixel position on the plan image, myseliasan's own record with a name snapshot so a marker stays visible even while its node is offline; a camera placement now also carries a Heading/Fov coverage arc (degrees), aimed via on-marker handles and drawn as a translucent wedge on the plan so an operator can see which part of a room a camera actually watches. A new GET /api/node-floorplan/{nodeId} drill-down backs a 'Locate on plan' action from a camera event on the geographic view, jumping straight to the right floor plan with the right marker focused; clicking a camera marker opens a small, draggable, resizable floating live-view window (with PTZ when supported) whose recent events open their recorded snapshot/clip in their own floating window, all toggling small/maximized without restarting the underlying WebRTC stream. New/extended endpoints, all AuthOnly and session-gated the same as every other myseliasan operator route: /api/basemap, /api/sites, /api/floors (incl. POST .../image replace and GET .../background), /api/placements (incl. heading/fov), /api/node-floorplan/{nodeId}, and PUT /api/nodes/{id}/position.
- **myseliasan**: Node mTLS certificate renewal on the myseliasan fleet control plane is now operator-gated per node instead of renewing automatically and silently forever. A new ManagedNode.AutoRenew flag (default off for newly adopted nodes) must be enabled by an operator, via a new toggle on the node's Details tab or PUT /api/nodes/{id}/auto-renew, before the control plane's Enroll endpoint will honour a renewal re-enrollment from that node (the node still authenticates with its existing pairing token and needs no claim code; only the control plane's willingness to sign changed). Initial enrollment right after adoption is never gated. A node whose operator never turns this on simply lets its certificate lapse on schedule, drops off the control channel, and goes lost -- a per-node dead-man's switch so a forgotten or decommissioned node falls out of the fleet without anyone having to remember to explicitly revoke it. The fleet-health 'certificate expiring' warning now fires only for nodes with auto-renew off (an auto-renew-on node's renewal will be honoured, so its approaching expiry is not actionable). Node certificate default lifetime was raised from 7 to 90 days (pairing.certTtlHours, shipped config.json now sets 2160) so an un-renewed node stays reachable for a meaningful window, and the control-plane cert-expiry warning window (pairing.renewBeforeHours) was raised to 336 hours (14 days) in the shipped config to match.
- **myseliasan**: Extends the myseliasan Fleet Map (shipped as a single-node-pin geographic view) into a building-oriented digital twin: a Site now carries its own Lat/Lon/MapPlaced and an operator-chosen icon glyph, so a building - not the appliance that happens to record its cameras - is what anchors cameras on the geographic map. GET /api/sites/overview rolls up every building for the map (worst status among the nodes with cameras inside it, plus per-camera unread-notification counts via a new GET /api/notifications/tally, so one node recording cameras in several buildings is never double-counted onto all of them). A node can be dragged onto a building (or picked from a 'needs a home' list) to record that it physically resides there (PUT /api/nodes/{id}/building, ManagedNode.SiteId); a building-resident node stops drawing its own map pin and is represented by its building's marker instead. Clicking a building marker opens a new building-scoped floor-plan drill-down (GET /api/sites/{id}/floorplans, BuildingFloorView) showing every floor with every camera on it regardless of which node owns each one, each marker/coverage-wedge colored and streamed by its own owning node - distinct from the existing node-scoped floor view. The indoor floor-plan editor gained a proper create/edit building dialog (name + curated emoji icon picker, replacing a bare name prompt), pan/zoom + a select/delete toolbar for the plan canvas, keyboard delete/escape for the selected marker, and flags a placement as a 'ghost marker' when its camera no longer exists on an online, reachable node. The camera live window can now open a recorded event's snapshot/clip inline over the same live panel (a Back button returns to live without tearing down the underlying WebRTC stream). The offline basemap moved from a single .pmtiles archive to a directory of region archives (GET /api/basemap/tiles/{name}, region bounds parsed straight out of each PMTiles header) so a fleet spanning disjoint areas is served as several region files instead of one planet-sized archive, and gained an optional, off-by-default on-demand region download (POST /api/basemap/download, gated behind an operator- or env-configured remote pmtiles source plus the pmtiles CLI tool) - the one action in this otherwise air-gapped app that can reach the internet, and only when explicitly configured.
- **mymatasan**: Added a source-camera filter dropdown to the mymatasan Notifications page, alongside the existing Unread/All and category-type chip filters. Picking a camera passes cameraId straight through to the already-camera-aware GET /api/notifications endpoint (the backend already supported this parameter; only the UI was missing it), narrowing the feed to that camera's own AI-detection and camera-health notifications - the only notification kinds that carry a cameraId, so machine-health/login-security rows are naturally excluded once a specific camera is picked. Useful on any install with more than a couple of cameras, where the unified feed otherwise interleaves every camera's events together.
- **myseliasan,mymatasan,myiotsan**: Fixes a gap where the myseliasan fleet control plane undercounted a node's notifications: it only relayed events pushed live while a node's control channel was connected, so anything the node raised during a disconnect was dropped with no backfill. Node builds (mymatasan and myiotsan) now accept a since=<unix seconds> query param on GET /api/notifications that returns that node's own notifications oldest-first from that time (domain/notification.Service.ListSince, capped 500/page); the persisted notification metadata now also carries the node's stable engine id (domain/notification.OriginIDKey = "__oid") so a pulled row and a live-pushed one share one dedup key. On myseliasan, ControlServer now fires an onConnect callback the moment a node's control connection is (re)accepted, which pulls that node's notifications from the last 72 hours over the tunnel and re-ingests anything missing; a new relayed_notif dedup ledger (services.RelayDedup) makes this idempotent against events already delivered live or by an earlier replay, and an hourly job prunes ledger rows outside twice the replay window. This is scoped to all three apps together (not just myseliasan) because the parent-side replay only works against a node build new enough to serve the since= endpoint -- deploying only the myseliasan half would silently no-op the replay against every existing node.

### Changed

- **core**: The r450k static marketing site content (r450k/src/content/*.js) has been refreshed to reflect the suite's latest capabilities. Two new feature cards were added: 'Fleet maps & floor plans' (the myseliasan control plane now maps sites/buildings and edge nodes geographically, with an in-app per-building floor-plan designer, camera placement with field-of-view coverage cones, a building view aggregating multiple nodes' cameras, and click-to-locate / live view from the map) and 'Face recognition' (mymatasan edge nodes enroll known people and turn a detection into a named alert, entirely on-prem). The 'Unified notification feed' card now mentions per-camera filtering to isolate one camera's events, and the 'Fleet pairing over the LAN' card now mentions operator-gated per-node mTLS certificate auto-renewal so fleet trust stays current without manual re-pairing. The 'Multi-site fleets' use case and the myseliasan app card were enriched with the map/floor-plan capabilities. Two new inline SVG icons (map, face) were added to r450k/src/components/Icon.jsx for the new cards. All copy was translated across en/ms/zh/ar (English, Malay, Simplified Chinese, Arabic/RTL) at full parity.

### Fixed

- **myseliasan**: Fixed two related node-adoption control-channel gaps that could leave a mymatasan/myiotsan node effectively unmanageable from the myseliasan control plane. First, if a node's adopt call succeeded but the control plane then failed to save the resulting ManagedNode row (a DB hiccup or schema mismatch), the node had already committed to pairing and burned its single-use claim code - it was left permanently stranded, invisible to discovery and unable to retry. NodeRegistry.Adopt now best-effort rolls back the node's own pairing in that case (so it becomes discoverable and adoptable again) and returns a new ErrAdoptPersist with an actionable operator-facing message instead of a raw database error, while the raw cause is still logged server-side. Second, a node that already holds a valid fleet-CA certificate but has no managed record (e.g. released here without being reset on the node) kept dialing the control channel and being silently refused forever, with no operator-visible symptom beyond a repeating log line. ControlServer now tracks each refusal (deduped by node id, bounded at 200 entries) and a new Unrecognized nodes panel in the Nodes UI lists them (GET /api/nodes/unrecognized) with Block (POST /api/nodes/{id}/block, revokes the certificate via a new RevokeNode registry method that needs no managed row) and Dismiss (POST /api/nodes/{id}/forget) actions. AcceptControlConn was also reordered to check certificate revocation before the row lookup, so a blocked-but-row-less node correctly reports as revoked rather than a generic unknown node that would make Block appear to do nothing.
- **myseliasan**: Fixed a bug where a camera's notification events could silently go missing from its floor-plan live window (CameraWindow) once that node had enough recent activity across all its cameras: the window fetched the owning node's whole notification feed (capped at 60 rows) and filtered to the one camera client-side, so a camera's older events fell off the page the moment other cameras on the same node generated enough notifications to push them past the 60-row limit - while the badge count (which tallies the full unread set server-side) kept showing the true, larger number, so the list and the badge visibly disagreed. GET /api/notifications now accepts a cameraId query parameter that scopes the list server-side (in addition to the existing nodeId scope). The floor-plan CameraWindow now reads the OWNING NODE's own notification feed for that camera live over the control-channel proxy (falling back to the control plane's relayed feed only when the node is unreachable), because the control plane holds only the notifications that streamed up while the channel was connected whereas the node keeps its full history - so the window shows the node's authoritative list instead of the relayed subset. Footage and snapshots already resolved from the node over the same proxy, so their ids line up.
## 2026-07-17 — mymatasan 1.109.0, core 1.63.0 (bd663c9)

### Added

- **mymatasan,infra**: Face recognition: a new global-gallery enroll->recognize feature, the detect-embed-match sibling of LPR, built as a stacked detection stage rather than the singleton custom-YOLO slot so it coexists with a trained model. Admins enroll named people once (People tab / POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /api/faces, one or more clear front-facing photos per person) into a gallery stored as encrypted embeddings (AES-256-GCM via the existing at-rest cipher, base64 TEXT so it stays portable across sqlite/MariaDB/Postgres); enrollment refuses an image with zero, multiple, low-quality, or too-small faces so a bad photo can never silently poison future matches. A new detection rule mode (detectionType: "face") fires on any recognized person (matchMode: known, the per-camera toggle's default), a chosen watchlist (include + a people list), or unrecognized strangers only (unknown), gated by a minConfidence floor (default 0.6) below which a marginal match is treated as unknown rather than risking naming the wrong person. Recognized identity (personId, personName, faceConfidence, recognized) is promoted to top-level alert metadata for notification templates, same pattern as LPR's plate metadata. The worker (OpenCV YuNet detect + SFace 128-d embed, both permissively licensed, both already-available-dependency opencv-python/numpy - zero new heavy dependency) shares the same high-resolution per-camera capture gate LPR already uses (WantFace, alongside WantLPR) so cameras without a face rule pay no extra capture or compute cost. setup.ps1 gained a -Faces switch that installs the Python deps and downloads the two .onnx model files.
## 2026-07-17 — myiotsan 0.20.0, mymatasan 1.108.0 (7409ee0)

### Added

- **myiotsan**: Shipped a three-phase home-automation surface for myiotsan, entirely on top of its existing gated actuation path. Phase 1: `entities.ProfileCommand.Kind` gained five values beyond switch/setpoint -- dimmer/position (a 0..100 percentage), cct (a Kelvin setpoint bounded by min/max), mode (one of the integer values a new `options` field enumerates as a JSON [{value,label}] list, itself refusing an empty/unlisted value the same way an unbounded setpoint refuses everything), and color (an RGB colour packed into one integer, 0xRRGGBB, carried through the existing single-float DeviceCommand/twin model via new packRGB/unpackRGB helpers, with renderPayload substituting {r}/{g}/{b} into the command's payload template). Along the way, `services.validateValue`'s kind switch gained a `default` case that REFUSES an unrecognised Kind -- closing a latent hole where an unknown or misconfigured kind was previously published to a device completely unvalidated. A new builtin `smart-lamp` profile (Zigbee2MQTT conventions: power/brightness/color_temp/color) is the worked example. Phase 2: new `Scene`/`SceneAction` entities and `services.SceneService` group an ordered set of device commands under one name ("goodnight", "movie night") and run them via `POST /api/scenes/{id}/run` (admin-only) by fanning out through the exact same `CommandService.Issue` a manual command takes, one call per action -- every gate (actuation-enabled, admin-only, declared-commands-only, server-side bounds, rate limit, audit, twin confirmation, never-auto-retry) applies per action, and a scene never rolls back or stops early on a refusal, reporting each action's outcome independently (verified live: a scene run's per-action report correctly surfaced a rate-limit refusal on one action while the rest still succeeded). Phase 3: new `Schedule` entity and `services.ScheduleService` fire a scene or a single command at a fixed time of day (optionally weekday-filtered) or at local sunrise/sunset plus or minus an offset -- the sun times computed locally via a new pure NOAA sunrise/sunset calculation (`services/sun.go`, no network dependency, verified against published almanac times for London and the Svalbard midsummer polar-day case) against a site latitude/longitude stored as a runtime setting (`GET/PUT /api/settings/location`, admin-only). A new `safego.Supervise`d `myiotsan.scheduler` task ticks once a minute, aligned to the minute boundary, and fires every due schedule through the identical actuation path, with a persisted `LastFiredAt` unix-minute double-fire guard (the same restart-survives-cooldown discipline IotRule's alert cooldown already applies, now applied to time) and a synthetic `schedule:<name>` firing actor so the audit trail attributes it rather than reading "System". New RBAC rows: /api/scenes and /api/schedules are readable by viewer/operator; running a scene, test-firing or authoring a schedule, and setting the site location are all admin-only, mirroring the line issuing a command already draws. Frontend gained a Scenes page and a Schedules page (schedule editor plus a location panel) under a new Automation nav group, plus slider/select/colour-picker widgets on the device Control tab for the new command kinds. Verified live end to end: booted the app, seeded smart-lamp, issued a dimmer command, ran a scene, set the site location, and created and test-fired a sunset schedule.
- **myiotsan**: Added a tabbed Settings page for myiotsan (`apps/myiotsan/views/react-webpack/src/views/components/settings.js`) consolidating everything that configures the hub itself -- Users, Location, Notifications, Telemetry, Connectivity, System -- behind one admin-only nav entry, backed by two new runtime-editable settings stores and a system-restart endpoint. `services.NotificationSettingsService` (new `apps/myiotsan/services/notification_settings.go`) persists a delivery config as a JSON blob in the shared RuntimeSetting KV and, critically, is the FIRST code path in the app that ever calls the shared `notification.Service.Configure` -- before this change myiotsan wrote every alert only to its in-app feed regardless of any config, because nothing wired the shared outbound delivery engine up at all. Delivery is now modeled per-DESTINATION rather than a fixed webhook+telegram pair: a new `services.NotificationDestination` (new `apps/myiotsan/services/notification_destination.go`, ported from mymatasan's destination model and scaled down -- the vision-only fields like snapshot mode and template tokens are dropped since a sensor alert has no bounding box or plate) is one delivery target of type webhook, telegram, or mqtt, each with its own minimum-severity floor and a category filter over the two categories myiotsan actually emits (`device.alert`, `system`; empty = all). `NotificationSettings.Destinations` is now authoritative; the original `Webhook`/`Telegram` singleton fields are kept only to migrate an older pre-destinations config forward once (`migrateLegacyDestinations`). New `PUT /api/settings/notification/destination` and `DELETE /api/settings/notification/destination/{id}` (in `apps/myiotsan/apis/settings.go`) upsert or remove one destination without clobbering the others; the existing `GET/PUT /api/settings/notification` and `POST /api/settings/notification/test` are unchanged in shape apart from carrying the new `destinations` array. Saving from the Notifications tab now applies the config to the live hub immediately via `Configure`, and a `Sync` call at boot re-applies a previously-saved config to each fresh process. The Notifications tab itself was rebuilt as an accordion of destinations (one row each, category checkboxes, MQTT broker/topic/QoS/retain/TLS fields where relevant) to match mymatasan's per-destination UI, reusing a newly-ported `AccordionList`/`AccordionItem` (`ui.js`) now shared by both the Notifications tab and AI-adjacent list editors. Alongside this, the whole Settings page was restructured from a single tabbed panel into mymatasan's topic/subtopic layout (a `settings-tab-head` band under the shared Tabs bar, then `settings-panel` subtopic cards), and an earlier UI-consistency regression was fixed suite-wide: an oversized custom `iot-checkbox` class (introduced with the original Settings page) was replaced everywhere with the canonical `check-row` class already used elsewhere in the app, including in `scenes.js` and `schedules.js`'s enabled toggles. `services.TelemetrySettingsService` (new `apps/myiotsan/services/telemetry_settings.go`) persists raw/rollup retention days, write-batcher batch size/flush interval/queue size, and the embedded MQTT broker's listen address (`GET/PUT /api/settings/telemetry`), seeded from `config.json` defaults the first time; `app.go` now reads this store's effective value once at boot (store-over-config) to size `NewReadingWriter`, `telemetry.RunRollup`, and `iotmqtt.New`, replacing direct `config.json` reads at those three call sites -- an edit here does not apply live and needs a restart, which the tab states plainly and links to. A new `apps/myiotsan/apis/system.go` (`NewSystemApi`) adds `POST /api/system/restart` (admin-only, responds 200 before restarting ~500ms later via the shared apphost.Restarter) to make that restart reachable from inside the app. The RBAC catalog (`apps/myiotsan/services/rbac.go`) gained explicit admin-only rows for `/api/settings/notification` (its existing prefix row already covers the new `/destination` sub-routes under the matrix's longest-prefix match), `/api/settings/telemetry`, and `/api/system`, and separately closed a pre-existing gap found alongside this work: `/api/pairing` (fleet key, claim code, adoption status) had never been listed in the catalog at all. The existing site-location panel moved from the Schedules page into the new Settings page (Location tab) with no backend change. Verified live end to end on a booted app: created a user and assigned it a role, set the site location, saved and test-fired a webhook (confirmed delivery was actually configured), saved telemetry retention (confirmed unedited fields kept their defaults), read pairing status, read version/health, then -- for the destinations work -- PUT a webhook destination with categories and a separate MQTT destination, GET reflected both, test-fired (Configure applied), and DELETE removed one destination while leaving the other intact.

Follow-up (frontend-only, no Go/API changes): fixed a CSS gap bug where the Telemetry tab's `<form>` wrapper around its panels lost the `.settings-content` flex column's inter-panel gap (added a matching `.settings-content > form` rule to `styles/iot.css`). Also redesigned the Settings > Users tab from a wall of inline-editable rows into a scannable `DataTable` (initials avatar + name/@username, colour-coded role badge, active/inactive status pill, pending-password-change indicator) with add/edit moved behind a modal dialog and reset-password folded into that same edit modal -- the same admin user-management pattern used elsewhere in the suite. Purely presentational: the same `/api/settings/users` endpoints are called with the same payload shapes.

Follow-up 2 (frontend-only, no Go/API changes): standardized role display across myiotsan so the stored role name `superadmin` always renders as "Administrator" -- matching mymatasan -- instead of the raw internal name; a new `roleLabel(t, name)` helper (`lib/helpers.js`) maps `superadmin`/`operator`/`viewer` to new `role.admin`/`role.operator`/`role.viewer` i18n keys (added to all four locales), falling back to the raw name for any custom role. Used in the side-nav account card and the Users tab's role badge/role picker (`components/layout.js`, `components/settings.js`). Also fixed a CSS gap bug on the Settings > Connectivity tab, where its "Save fleet key" actions row sat flush under the field grid above it with no spacing (`.settings-field-grid + .settings-actions { margin-top: 14px; }` added to `styles/iot.css`). Purely presentational -- no backend, RBAC, or API change; role names and permissions are unchanged, only their on-screen label.
- **myiotsan**: Added a Flow Engine to myiotsan: a Node-RED-style visual, executable data-flow canvas for wiring telemetry into computed values, alerts, and (guarded) actuation, shipped across four phases (P1 engine, P2 canvas, P3 templates + derived metric, P4 rate-limiting + MQTT bridge-out + editor polish). A flow is a new entity (`IotFlow`, `apps/myiotsan/entities/iot_flow.go`) storing a node/wire graph as one JSON document, authored on a new admin-only frontend page (`apps/myiotsan/views/react-webpack/src/views/components/flows.js`): a flow list plus an SVG drag-and-drop canvas (palette, wiring, per-node config panel, zoom), reachable from a new "Flows" entry under the Automation nav group and new i18n keys (`flows.*`) in all four locales. The node palette (`apps/myiotsan/services/flows.go`) covers an input (`device_telemetry`, emits on a new reading), transforms (`scale`, and `function`/`expression`/`switch` -- arbitrary JavaScript), logic (`threshold`, `deadband`, and `throttle` (P4) -- passes at most once per N seconds, dropping anything inside the window), and outputs (`debug` for the live inspector, `notify` to raise an alert, `command` to actuate, `derived_metric` (P3) to persist a computed value as a new telemetry series, and `mqtt_out` (P4) to publish the payload to an MQTT topic via the embedded broker -- data outward, never a device command, so it does not go through the actuation gate). `parseGraph` validates every graph at save and again at compile: closed node-type set, no dangling wires, no cycles, a 200-node sanity ceiling. `services.FlowRuntime` (new `apps/myiotsan/services/flow_runtime.go`) compiles a graph and runs it on its own worker goroutine, tapping the SAME decoded-sample stream `RuleService.OnReading` does (new `Ingest.SetFlows`/`ingest.go`'s `handleSamples` tap) without ever blocking ingest (events are dropped under backpressure, counted, never queued unboundedly), and reconciling compiled flows against the database on a ticker plus an explicit change signal so an edit/enable/disable takes effect with no restart -- the same pattern the Modbus poller already uses. The `function`/`expression`/`switch` nodes run in a brand-new embedded, sandboxed JavaScript interpreter (`github.com/dop251/goja`, new pure-Go dependency, isolated in `apps/myiotsan/services/flow_eval.go`) with NO host bindings at all (no filesystem, network, `require`, or `os`) and a hard 100ms-per-call watchdog that interrupts a runaway script -- both guarantees are pinned by tests that try to escape the sandbox and that drive an infinite loop. The safety invariant that makes 'a flow can run arbitrary JavaScript' acceptable: only the dedicated `command` output node can actuate, and it routes through the SAME guarded `CommandService.Issue` chokepoint every manual command, scene, and schedule already uses (actuation-enabled, admin-only, declared bounds, rate limit, full audit, never auto-retried) -- an arbitrary-JS node can shape a *value* but cannot skip a gate, retry a refusal, or reach a device the command layer would otherwise refuse. This is a deliberate, later reversal of `docs/MYIOTSAN_PLAN.md` §8g's original 'no visual node-graph editor' scope line (now documented as §8i, with the reversal called out at §8g and in §9's risk table).
