P3 added slot-based templates -- a device named by a placeholder (`"$inverter"`) instead of a concrete key makes a flow reusable; `GET /api/flows/{id}/slots` reports what a flow declares and `POST /api/flows/{id}/instantiate` binds every slot to a real device, stamping out a concrete, disabled-by-default copy for an admin to review and enable -- and a shipped builtin 'Solar system' template flow (`apps/myiotsan/services/flow_catalog.go`, seeded idempotently at boot like a device profile) demonstrating the one thing the data-flow model exists to do: combine two telemetry streams (grid + PV power) into a derived self-consumption value via the per-flow JS context, plus a high-grid-import threshold alert. Flow import/export (`.iotflow`, `apps/myiotsan/services/flow_transfer.go`) mirrors the existing `.iotprofile` pattern: an import is never builtin and always arrives disabled. New API (`apps/myiotsan/apis/flows.go`): `GET/POST /api/flows`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/flows/{id}`, `POST /api/flows/import`, `GET /api/flows/{id}/export`, `GET /api/flows/{id}/slots`, `POST /api/flows/{id}/instantiate`, `POST /api/flows/{id}/run` (test-fires the flow off the live runtime with a synthetic seed value -- an output node still acts for real, which is the point), and `GET /api/flows/{id}/debug` (the live per-node inspector snapshot). The whole `/api/flows` area is admin-only in the RBAC matrix (`apps/myiotsan/services/rbac.go`) -- unlike scenes/schedules, even READING a flow's graph is denied below admin, because the graph itself can reveal an actuation path. Verified with a dedicated unit suite (`apps/myiotsan/services/flows_test.go`) covering propagation through scale/threshold/deadband, the two JS node types (including the per-flow context surviving across two separate input messages, the mechanism the solar sample depends on), sandbox containment (require/process/filesystem all fail), the watchdog killing a `while(true){}` node within a bounded window, guarded-issuer-only actuation, notification publishing, graph validation rejections, the derived-metric output, and slot detection/substitution/template guards. P4 is a polish pass on the same engine, all in the existing flow files: the `throttle` logic node above is stateful (`compiledFlow.lastPass`, keyed like `deadband`'s state) but deliberately timer-free -- it only ever drops a message inside its window, never defers or replays one later, so it cannot itself become a delayed loop. The `mqtt_out` output node above reaches the broker through a new `mqttPublish` dep (`NewFlowRuntime` gained a `publish` parameter, wired from `app.go` as `broker.Publish`) and renders the payload as its shortest decimal for a number, as-is for a string, or JSON otherwise. `cfgFloat` (`services/flows.go`) was fixed to also parse a string-encoded config value -- a `<select>` field (e.g. `mqtt_out`'s QoS picker) stores its option value as text -- falling back to `strconv.ParseFloat`; payload coercion stays strict, only config reading is this lenient. Two new hermetic unit tests cover both nodes (`TestFlow_ThrottleDropsWithinWindow`, `TestFlow_MqttOutPublishesPayload` against a fake publisher). The canvas (`components/flows.js`) gained editor ergonomics: Delete/Backspace removes the selected node or wire (guarded against firing while a text field has focus); non-blocking validation warnings surface directly on the canvas for a node with an unwired port or missing required config (empty code, no device+key, no topic) as an amber dashed border and dot, a toolbar count badge, and a matching banner in the config panel, so a half-wired flow is visible before saving rather than discovered only at test-fire time; a reset-view toolbar button restores the default pan/zoom; and a new `bool` config-field kind renders the `mqtt_out` retain flag as a checkbox. New `flows.node.throttle`/`flows.node.mqttOut`, `flows.f.seconds`/`flows.f.topic`/`flows.f.qos`/`flows.f.retain`, `flows.resetView`/`flows.warnHint`, and `flows.warn.*` i18n keys were added to all four locales (en/ms/zh/ar). The server's hard `parseGraph` validation (unknown types, cycles, dangling wires) is unchanged and remains the last word at save.
- **myiotsan**: Made Modbus TCP symmetric with MQTT for actuation, and gave the profile/device editor a Modbus-aware authoring UI. `entities.ProfileCommand` gained `Transport` (""/"mqtt" default, or "modbus"), `Register`, `RegKind` (u16/i16), and `ScaleFactor`. `CommandService.Issue` now branches to a new `sendModbus` when a command's transport is Modbus, AFTER every existing gate (read-only-by-default, admin-only, declared bounds, rate limit, audit, never-auto-retried) -- Modbus actuation inherits the identical safety gates MQTT commands have always gone through, it is not a parallel, looser path. `sendModbus` encodes the value via a new `encodeRegister` helper (applies the read-side scale in reverse: raw = round(value / scaleFactor); range-checks against the declared RegKind; only single-register u16/i16 are written, u32/i32 are refused rather than half-written) and performs the write through a `modbusWrite` seam (production = `infra/iot/modbus.WriteConfirm`, a guarded write-then-read-back that never re-issues, bounded by a new 5s `modbusWriteTimeout`; a fake in tests). A successful guarded write sets the command straight to `confirmed` (stronger than MQTT's `sent`, since the device already reported the value back) rather than waiting on a separately-reported telemetry reading; a lost/unconfirmed write ends the command `failed`, audited, exactly like an MQTT refusal, and is never retried. `services.SaveProfileCommand` and `ProfileService.replaceCommands` carry the four new fields through the profile CRUD path unchanged in shape from the pre-existing MQTT fields. Separately, the backend already supported custom Modbus device profiles (Transport/ModbusMode/ModbusBase/PollSeconds, per-key Register/RegKind/ScaleFactor/WordSwap, device Endpoint/Unit) from the prior SunSpec driver-foundation work, but the frontend profile editor was MQTT-shaped only; it is now transport-aware: a Transport toggle (MQTT / Modbus TCP) on the profile editor reveals Modbus mode (SunSpec auto-discover vs. an explicit register map), base register and poll-seconds fields; each telemetry key's JSON-path input becomes a register/regKind/scaleFactor/word-swap binding for a register-map profile; and each command's MQTT topic/payload/confirm-key inputs become a register/regKind/scaleFactor write binding (no confirm-key needed -- the write confirms itself by read-back). The device create/edit forms now show an Endpoint (host:port) + Unit id field in place of the broker password when the chosen profile is Modbus. New i18n keys added across en/ms/zh/ar for all of the above.
- **myiotsan**: Shipped three more built-in Modbus device profiles for the solar hardware installers actually buy outside the SolarEdge/SMA/Fronius/Huawei world: sungrow-sh-hybrid (Sungrow SH residential hybrids -- #2 in global inverter shipments), deye-hybrid (one map covering Deye, Sunsynk, and Sol-Ark rebadges), and eastron-sdm630-meter (a read-only 3-phase meter). Sungrow and Deye also pre-declare Modbus commands (ems_mode/batt_force/batt_force_power/export_limit/export_limit_enable/batt_min_soc/batt_max_soc for Sungrow; work_mode/solar_sell/grid_charge/max_sell_power for Deye) -- the first built-in profiles to do so -- though every command stays inert until an admin turns on the device's actuation AND bench-verifies the register, since sign/scale/units differ by firmware. This required two additions to the Modbus register-map driver (infra/iot/modbus/regmap.go): an optional inputReader interface + Point.Input so a map can bind INPUT registers (Modbus fn 4) alongside the existing holding-register (fn 3) path -- clusters now partition by bank first, since the two function codes can never share a round trip -- and a new PF32 PType decoding IEEE-754 float32 (the encoding cheap meters like the Eastron use). entities.TelemetryKey gained a matching RegInput field and "f32" became a valid RegKind, threaded through the profile CRUD/import-export/builtin-seed paths and the frontend profile editor (a new "Input register (fn 4)" toggle per telemetry key). Five new built-in Flow Engine templates ship alongside these on the $inverter slot: solar-self-consumption and solar-self-sufficiency (derived series persisted for charts/rollups), solar-battery-guard (low-SoC alert + a guarded force-charge command below a critical floor), solar-export-limit (caps grid export via a guarded command -- the control showcase), and solar-inverter-health (overheat + abnormal-running-state alerts). Finally, a new in-app knowledge base (apps/myiotsan/kb, served at GET /api/kb and GET /api/kb/{slug}, readable by viewer and operator as well as admin) ships eight compiled-in Markdown setup articles covering every new profile, the SunSpec-native path, Modbus TCP vs RTU-over-TCP, an RS485-to-TCP gateway how-to, and how to verify a control register before enabling actuation -- surfaced in the frontend as a new Help page with a dependency-free Markdown renderer. Live-boot verified: the knowledge base is served, all profiles and flow templates seed correctly, and the FC04/float32/word-swap read path decodes correctly against a Modbus mock.
- **myiotsan**: Added Modbus RTU (serial) and RTU-over-TCP transports, so a Modbus device no longer has to be reachable over Modbus TCP/MBAP. infra/iot/modbus gained a transport abstraction: a shared `transport` interface splits the original MBAP-over-TCP framing (`mbapTransport`, moved out of client.go with no behavior change; `Dial` is unchanged) from a new RTU framing (`rtuTransport`, transport.go) used by both a serial line and a raw TCP socket -- RTU has no length header, so the response size is inferred from the request's function code, and every frame is checked against a Modbus RTU CRC-16 (new crc.go: crc16/appendCRC/crcOK). A new `DialRTUTCP` opens RTU framing over a plain TCP socket, for transparent RS485-to-TCP gateways that don't speak real Modbus TCP. A new serial.go adds `SerialParams` (baud/parity/data-bits/stop-bits, defaulting to the near-universal 9600 8N1), `DialSerial`, and a per-port mutex registry so several unit ids sharing one physical RS485 bus are polled one at a time rather than colliding -- backed by a new pure-Go dependency, go.bug.st/serial v1.8.0 (no cgo, keeping the single-binary/air-gapped posture). `DeviceConf` gained `Transport` (TCP/RTU-over-TCP/Serial) and `Serial SerialParams`, and a `dial()` helper both `PollOnce` and `WriteConfirm` now use, so SunSpec auto-discovery, register-map reads, and guarded control writes all work unchanged over any of the three transports. On the app side, `entities.IotDevice` gained `Transport`/`Baud`/`Parity`/`DataBits`/`StopBits` (for serial, Endpoint holds the port name -- COM3, /dev/ttyUSB0 -- instead of host:port), plumbed through device create/update requests and a new `modbusTransportOf` helper the poller's `planFor` uses to build each device's `DeviceConf`. The guarded-write seam in CommandService (`modbusWriteFunc`) changed shape from a bare (endpoint, unit, reg, value, timeout) to (modbus.DeviceConf, reg, value, timeout) so a control write inherits the device's transport exactly like a poll does, and now wires directly to modbus.WriteConfirm with no wrapper needed. The frontend device editor gained a shared ModbusFields component: a Transport select (Modbus TCP / RTU over TCP / Serial) plus, for serial, port name and baud/parity/data-bits/stop-bits fields, with new en i18n keys.
- **myiotsan**: Added active network discovery scanning to myiotsan, the counterpart to the existing MQTT enrollment window: an admin can now sweep the LAN instead of only waiting for a device to announce itself. New package infra/iot/discover normalises every scanner to a shared Found type: ScanModbus (a gentle subnet sweep -- a cheap TCP :502 connect probe before it ever walks unit ids on a host, then SunSpec auto-identification via the existing sunspec.Discover for vendor/model/serial, falling back to an 'unidentified Modbus' candidate when a device answers but isn't SunSpec-compliant), ScanMDNS (browses common DNS-SD service types via a new pure-Go dependency, github.com/hashicorp/mdns, pulling in github.com/miekg/dns -- Chromecast/HomeKit/Shelly/printers etc.), ScanSSDP (UDP 1900 M-SEARCH for Sonos/Roku/DLNA), ScanEtherNetIP (UDP 44818 ListIdentity broadcast for Rockwell/CIP PLCs), and ScanBACnet (UDP 47808 Who-Is broadcast for building-automation controllers) -- plus a Hosts helper that expands a CIDR into a flat host list capped at 1024 addresses so a fat subnet mask can never launch a runaway sweep. On the app side, a new ScanService (apps/myiotsan/services/scanner.go) orchestrates the requested scanners and records every Found as the SAME quarantined DiscoveredDevice candidate the MQTT announce path already produces -- a scan never adds a device, it only proposes candidates an admin then adopts through the identical review step, deduped by a synthetic device key so re-scanning refreshes rather than duplicates, and audited to the notification feed exactly like opening an enrollment window. DiscoveredDevice gained Source/Address/Endpoint/Unit/Transport fields, and adopting a Modbus-scan candidate now carries its connection straight into the created device (services.Enrollment.Adopt) so it starts polling immediately with no manual re-typing. New route POST /api/discovery/scan (admin-only, under the already-admin-only /discovery prefix). Frontend gained a Scan panel on the Discovery page (per-protocol toggles plus CIDR/transport/units for the Modbus sweep) and a scan-aware candidate card showing how a candidate was found and its address instead of a topic. Every scanner is opt-in (admin-triggered), bounded (host cap, per-scan timeout, concurrency cap), strictly read-only (no scanner ever writes to a device), and LAN-local. Verified live end to end for Modbus/mDNS/SSDP (scan -> SunSpec-identify -> candidate with endpoint/unit prefilled and generic-sunspec-solar suggested -> adopt -> device; mDNS/SSDP ran against a real LAN without error); EtherNet/IP and BACnet are parser-verified only against synthetic protocol-mock byte frames, since no real PLC was available in CI -- see docs/DISCOVERY_SCANNING.md for the honest per-scanner verification status and what was deliberately deferred (OPC-UA discovery, Profinet DCP, a Matter controller, native TV/AV control) and why. Alongside this, a small label fix: the profile editor's Transport option now reads 'Modbus' instead of 'Modbus TCP', and its hint clarifies that the connection (Modbus TCP / RTU-over-TCP / serial) is chosen per device, since that axis is really the protocol family (MQTT vs Modbus), not the wire transport.

### Changed

- **mymatasan**: Redesigned the Settings → Users tab to match myiotsan's admin user-management pattern: a scannable `DataTable` (initials avatar + name/@username, a colour-coded role badge, an active/inactive status pill, and a pending-password-change indicator) replaces the old two-panel layout of a separate "add user" form plus a wall of inline-editable user cards. Add and edit now happen behind a modal dialog (`UserModal`), with reset-password folded into the same edit modal instead of a per-card inline field. The new `UsersManager` component (in `apps/mymatasan/views/react-webpack/src/views/components/settings.js`) is self-contained — it owns its own fetch/state, following the same pattern already used by `StockModelPanel` — so the now-dead user-CRUD plumbing (`users`/`newUser`/`passwordDrafts` state, `loadUsers`/`createUser`/`editUser`/`updateUser`/`resetUserPassword`/`deleteUser` handlers, and their props into `SettingsTab`) was removed from `App.js`. New i18n keys (`st.usersHint`, `st.user`, `st.status`, `st.editUser`, `st.activeAccount`, `st.resetPwHint`, `st.deleteUserTitle`, `st.deleteUserBody`) were added to all four locales (en/ms/zh/ar). The old `.user-card`/`.user-list` CSS was replaced with new table/badge/pill/modal styles in `app.css`. Purely presentational — no backend or API changes; the same `/api/settings/users` (list/create/update/delete) and `/api/settings/users/{id}/password` endpoints are called with the same payload shapes.
- **myiotsan**: Moved myiotsan device onboarding (the MQTT enrollment window, the network scan, and the candidate/adopt flow) from a standalone admin-only "Discovery" entry under the System nav group to an admin-only "Discover" sub-tab under the Devices page, alongside a new "Inventory" sub-tab (the device list plus manual "Add device") -- a frontend-only navigation change, no API or backend behavior moved. New apps/myiotsan/views/react-webpack/src/views/components/deviceshome.js exports DevicesHome, a controlled two-tab wrapper (built on the shared @shared/Tabs) that renders the existing, unchanged DevicesPage under Inventory and the existing, unchanged DiscoveryPage under Discover; App.js now renders DevicesHome for the devices tab (lifting a devicesSub state so the first-run wizard's "go set up a device" action can land directly on Discover), and the standalone discovery nav entry/route/import were removed from layout.js and App.js. Rationale: onboarding a device is something an operator looks for under "Devices", not as a separate System-level page.
## 2026-07-15 — myiotsan 0.12.0 (6981699)

### Added

- **myiotsan**: Wired the Modbus/SunSpec driver foundation (previously a standalone, unwired library) into the running myiotsan app, and shipped its first two device profiles -- the P5 app-integration follow-up to the just-landed driver foundation. `entities.TelemetryKey` gained a Modbus binding (`register`/`regKind` u16|i16|u32|i32/`scaleFactor`/`wordSwap`); `entities.DeviceProfile` gained `transport` (mqtt default, or modbus), `modbusMode` (sunspec self-describing, or regmap explicit vendor map), `modbusBase`, and `pollSeconds`; `entities.IotDevice` gained `endpoint`/`unit` for a device the app dials OUT to. `services.Ingest.Handle`'s back half (deadband -> storage -> rules -> twin) was extracted into `handleSamples`, and a new `Ingest.HandlePolled` feeds it from a poll driver with no MQTT payload to parse and no enrollment quarantine to apply. A new `services.ModbusPoller` runs one goroutine per Modbus device, reconciled against the device inventory on a 30s ticker (so a device added/edited/disabled in the UI starts, restarts, or stops its poller with no process restart), dialing out and decoding via SunSpec auto-discovery or a register map built from the profile's declared telemetry keys, feeding `HandlePolled`. The catalog gained two builtin profiles: `generic-sunspec-solar` (self-describing, reads any compliant inverter/meter/battery with zero per-model work) and `huawei-sun2000` (an explicit register-map worked example for the world's most-installed inverter). Building the Huawei map surfaced a real gap: its inverter (~32000), battery (~37760), and meter (~37113) register blocks sit ~5,700 registers apart -- far past the Modbus 125-register single-read limit -- so `infra/iot/modbus.RegisterMap.Read` gained clustered reads (one bounded request per block) instead of the single wide span it previously assumed; `tools/sunspec-sim` gained a fourth simulator persona (unit 4, the Huawei layout) to exercise this without hardware. Verified live: the app booted against the simulator, seeded both new profiles, polled unit 4 over Modbus TCP, and stored correctly scaled and signed readings (grid frequency 49.99 Hz, battery SOC 13.2%, grid +600W import).
## 2026-07-15 — myiotsan 0.11.0 (4639167)

### Added

- **myiotsan**: Added a Modbus/SunSpec driver foundation for myiotsan's planned solar-system support (P5), plus a standalone simulator to develop and prove it without real hardware. `infra/iot/sunspec` decodes the SunSpec information model that rides on Modbus: it walks a device's self-describing model chain (Common/Inverter/Meter/Storage/Controls), applies each field's scale-factor arithmetic, and role-prefixes every emitted key (`inv_ac_power`, `grid_ac_power`, `batt_soc`, `ctl_w_max_lim_pct`) so a hybrid inverter's own inverter block and its built-in grid meter never collide -- one driver decodes any compliant inverter/meter/battery with no per-vendor register map. `infra/iot/modbus` adds a stdlib Modbus TCP client (read holding/input, write single/multiple), a `RegisterMap` for the many cheaper, non-SunSpec-compliant devices that expose a flat vendor register block instead, a `Poller` that normalises either path to the same `codec.Sample` shape the MQTT ingest path already produces, and a guarded `WriteConfirm` that writes a register then confirms by read-back and never re-issues the write -- a Modbus write to an inverter or battery is a physical action, so a lost confirmation must not become a second one, mirroring the rule already enforced on the MQTT actuation path. `tools/sunspec-sim` is a new stdlib SunSpec-over-Modbus-TCP simulator serving three devices on three Modbus unit ids (a SunSpec hybrid inverter, a standalone SunSpec meter, and a non-SunSpec vendor inverter) driven by a live physics loop (PV bell curve, battery charge/discharge, grid balance, curtailment), so the mixed-protocol case a real solar system presents can be developed and tested without hardware; its control registers are honoured so a guarded write's read-back genuinely changes. Verified live: the driver read all three simulated personas and confirmed a curtailment write by read-back over a real Modbus TCP connection, in addition to hermetic unit tests covering the decoding and scaling deterministically. This is design and driver foundation only -- no app wiring: `device_profile`/`telemetry_key` still only understand a JSON-path binding, nothing in `app.go` polls Modbus yet, and the solar "system workspace" that would group an inverter+battery+meters into one monitored system (P8) remains design-only. See `docs/MYIOTSAN_PLAN.md` section 8g.
## 2026-07-15 — myidsan 1.13.0, myiotsan 0.10.0, mymatasan 1.107.0, myseliasan 1.32.0 (e49032f)

### Changed

- **mymatasan,myseliasan,myiotsan,myidsan**: Code-split each app's React i18n translation tables by locale instead of bundling all four dictionaries (en/ms/zh/ar) into the main JS chunk. Each app's views/i18n.js was rewritten from a static { en, ms, zh, ar } export to a tiny loader: it ships English eagerly (import en from './i18n/en'), since English is every key's fallback and must be present on first paint, and exposes loadLocaleDict(lang), which dynamically import()s ms/zh/ar from their own modules (views/i18n/{ms,zh,ar}.js) as separate Webpack chunks (i18n-ms/i18n-zh/i18n-ar) only when a user actually selects that language. Each app's App.js now holds an appMessages dictionary that starts at just the English bundle and accumulates locale chunks as they're loaded, plus a langReady gate that briefly withholds first paint for a returning non-English user until their saved locale's chunk has arrived (English users never wait), and an async changeLang that awaits the target locale's chunk before switching so the UI never flashes English mid-transition. Net effect on each app's initial download: ~308KB removed for mymatasan, ~340KB for myseliasan, ~112KB for myiotsan, ~40KB for myidsan -- translations for languages a user never selects are no longer fetched at all. No translation strings were added, removed, or reworded; this is a pure loading/bundling refactor. Verified live on mymatasan and myseliasan: an English-first load fetches zero locale chunks, switching to Chinese fetches only i18n-zh and renders with no English flash, and Arabic still sets dir=rtl correctly.
## 2026-07-15 — myseliasan 1.31.1 (9218dc2)

### Fixed

- **myseliasan**: The nodes page no longer renders a failed fleet load as an empty fleet. A network error or 5xx while loading /api/nodes now shows 'could not load the fleet — your session may have expired' with a retry link, instead of 'No adopted nodes yet' — which had told an operator whose control plane briefly restarted that their whole fleet had vanished. A failed refresh also no longer blanks a fleet that was already loaded.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.9.0, mymatasan 1.106.0, myseliasan 1.31.0 (0d0b789)

### Added

- **mymatasan,myseliasan,myiotsan**: The suite's /metrics endpoint exposed almost nothing app-specific -- a live scrape of mymatasan showed 3 series, and myiotsan and myseliasan showed 0. This adds the missing instrumentation across all four apps under one rule: instrument what FAILS SILENTLY, not what a log line an operator would already read covers. Shared infra/safego now routes every recovered panic in a supervised or one-shot background task to a new PanicObserver, and infra/apphost wires it for every app as <app>_task_panics_total{task} -- a supervised goroutine that panics is caught and restarted (the process stays up, the API answers) but until now left no other trace than a single log line, so a subsystem crash-looping for an hour was an incident-time discovery rather than something alertable. myiotsan gains 9 metrics (services/metrics.go): sampled ingest gauges (received/stored/suppressed/dropped/queue_depth/series) read off ingest's own atomic counters every 10s rather than instrumented on the hot publish path, device online/offline gauges, and a commands_total counter by outcome. The headline is myiotsan_ingest_dropped_total -- a reading shed because the write queue was full is silent data loss (the broker keeps accepting, the UI keeps rendering, nothing is logged); verified live at dropped_total 86 against a torrent that outran the disk. myseliasan gains 5 metrics (services/metrics.go): nodes_connected/nodes_adopted/control_channel_up sampled off the control server every 10s, plus fleet_events_total{kind} and fleet_rule_fired_total{severity} counted directly at their sink/fire sites -- because a control plane is a relay with no sensors of its own, a node dropping off the control channel looks in the UI identical to one an operator released on purpose, and a cert creeping toward expiry has no symptom until the day it expires.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.8.0, myseliasan 1.30.0 (2cea4c9)

### Added

- **myseliasan,myiotsan**: myseliasan can now manage an adopted myiotsan sensor node directly from its own UI: a new nodeiot/ embed (mirroring the existing nodecam camera-node embed) renders that node's Devices/Rules/Alerts/Discovery/Commands pages inside myseliasan, tunneled entirely through the existing commander proxy (/api/nodes/{id}/proxy/...) so the browser never opens a connection to the node itself. Alongside this, myiotsan's device_command and alert_event audit rows now record the acting operator's name (RequestedByName / AckedByName), not just a local user id — a command or acknowledgement issued from the myseliasan control plane arrives with no account on the node (id 0), so without the name the node's own audit trail would say a relay was switched or an alert acknowledged by "System" instead of the person who actually did it.

### Changed

- **myiotsan**: Package myiotsan as an installable product: portable archives, .deb/.rpm, Windows Inno installer, multi-arch Docker images, and CI that tags and releases it under its own myiotsan-v<ver> namespace
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.6.0, mymatasan 1.105.0, myseliasan 1.29.0 (7167a23)

### Added

- **mymatasan,myseliasan,myiotsan**: myiotsan is now adoptable into a myseliasan fleet on the same node-side stack mymatasan uses (LAN discovery, claim-code adoption, mTLS enrollment, the node-dialed control channel, and the event sink), which moved to a shared domain/shared/fleetnode package rather than being copied a second time. An adopted node now carries an authoritative Kind (camera or sensor hub), set from the fleet-key-signed adopt reply, not from the unsigned LAN discovery hint. With events flowing in from both camera and IoT nodes, myseliasan can now evaluate cross-domain fleet rules (a new Fleet rules page, superadmin-authored) that correlate across nodes no single node can see together, e.g. motion on a camera AND a door contact opening AND no badge swipe -> intrusion. A rule never fires on an event: it arms once its required clauses are satisfied, waits out a configurable grace period, and only then checks whether an absent condition (like a badge swipe) still holds, so a slightly-late but legitimate badge swipe correctly disarms it instead of raising a false alarm. Verified live end-to-end with a real adopted camera node and a real adopted sensor node, including a deliberately late badge swipe that correctly stayed silent.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.5.0 (291f2da)

### Added

- **myiotsan**: Shipped myiotsan's P4 (actuation & device twin): an admin can now WRITE to a device, not just read it — switching a relay or setting a bounded setpoint, currently for the one shipped profile that declares any command (smart-relay). Every safety gate the plan called for is enforced server-side in the new services.CommandService: read-only by default (a device is refused unless its ActuationEnabled toggle is explicitly on; adoption never sets it), admin-only (a catalog rule written in P0, before the command path existed), only what the device's profile declares (there is no generic publish-to-any-topic endpoint), server-side Min/Max bounds (a setpoint declaring no range at all, Min == Max == 0, refuses every value rather than allowing anything), a 2-second per-device rate limit, and a device_command audit row plus a notification for every attempt including refusals. Two properties the original plan did not name were also built in: a command is NEVER automatically retried after an unconfirmed timeout (30s, swept every 10s) because re-sending a relay write is a second physical action that could fire the relay twice, so a timeout ends the command with an explicit not-retried explanation and leaves the decision to a human (verified live against a relay simulator that obeys but never reports back: the relay physically switched, the command was recorded failed, and exactly one command was ever sent); and the device twin's desired state expires after 5 minutes and is never re-applied when a device reconnects, so a door controller offline for a month does not spring back to life and apply a stale unlock command nobody is around to see — the disagreement is still shown to an operator, just not acted on. A command reaches confirmed only when the device itself reports the resulting state back on the profile's declared confirm key (services.Ingest now feeds every decoded sample into the twin's reported half); sent means only that a message was published, not that anything physically happened, and the API/UI are both built around never conflating the two. Along the way, a real pre-existing gap was found and closed: /api/settings/users and /api/settings/roles had been named in myiotsan's policy catalog since P0, and the viewer/operator roles have existed since then, but nothing served those routes, so both roles were unassignable and the appliance was effectively single-admin; apps/myiotsan/apis/settings.go now serves them on the same shared appliance user service mymatasan uses. Reading a device's command history and its twin is granted to viewer and operator, not just admin, since seeing what was done to a device is not the same power as doing it.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.4.0 (b901cdd)

### Added

- **myiotsan**: Shipped myiotsan's P3 (discovery & onboarding), resolving the tension between the embedded broker's device-table-is-the-credential-store security model and onboarding a building's worth of sensors without typing each device key in by hand. An admin now opens a time-boxed enrollment window (POST /api/discovery/window, admin-only, TTL clamped to 1 hour, default 10 minutes) that mints a one-time key returned exactly once and bcrypt-hashed at rest; re-opening replaces it so only one key can ever be valid. An unknown MQTT client presenting that key is admitted but QUARANTINED (infra/iot/mqtt.Principal.Enrolling) — services.Ingest.Handle short-circuits for it at the very top of the pipeline, recording the payload as a discovered_device candidate (capped at 500) and doing nothing else: no telemetry row is written and no rule is evaluated, so an attacker who slips into the window can leave junk candidates for an admin to decline but cannot forge a reading, move a chart, or trigger/suppress an alert. A disabled device does not fall through to enrollment. Opening a window publishes a security event to the notification feed. An admin reviews candidates (GET /api/discovery/candidates, chattiest first), each carrying a device-type suggestion scored off the fraction of a profile's own telemetry keys the observed payload matched (a 0.6 floor — below it the app suggests nothing rather than guess wrong; verified live against a real temperature/humidity payload), and adopts (POST /api/discovery/candidates/{id}/adopt) or rejects it; adoption mints the device its own real, permanently generated credential and the enrollment key stops admitting it from that point on. Device-type profiles are now portable (GET /api/profiles/{id}/export, POST /api/profiles/import): an imported profile is never marked builtin regardless of what the document claims, and a slug collision is reported rather than silently overwriting the existing profile's decoding rules, so an integrator can carry a tuned deadband from one site to the next without risking a re-point of an in-use profile. The frontend gained a Discovery page and a first-run onboarding wizard that lead with enrollment as the primary onboarding path. mDNS/SSDP/portscan discovery and a Modbus TCP scan, both originally scoped to P3, were deliberately deferred: MQTT sensors announce over MQTT, not mDNS, so a network scan would mostly find gateways rather than sensors, and the Modbus scan belongs with the Modbus poller in P5 where it can be tested against a real device.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.3.0, core 1.62.0 (9131666)

### Added

- **myiotsan,core**: Shipped myiotsan's P1 (ingest spine) and P2 (rules & alerts) as one change set — P0-P2 is the shippable MVP. The app now ingests telemetry from real devices over a new embedded MQTT broker (infra/iot/mqtt, port 1883) whose authenticator is the device inventory itself, decodes payloads against a seven-profile built-in device-type catalog (door/window contact, PIR motion, temperature/humidity, smoke/heat, water leak, power meter, access reader), and persists readings through a per-key deadband gate that stores a row only when a value actually moves or a heartbeat elapses. Measured on a live appliance: 20 devices publishing ~30,000 samples in under a second produced 540 written rows, 98.2% suppressed by the deadband, zero dropped, settling the SQLite-write-throughput risk flagged in docs/MYIOTSAN_PLAN.md as the one thing that could invalidate the storage design; no time-series database was added. A ported rule engine (threshold/delta/rate/stuck/offline conditions, debounce, hysteresis, and a restart-surviving cooldown re-seeded from the alert log) evaluates every decoded sample, including ones the deadband suppressed, since the deadband is a storage decision and not a detection one; rule state is keyed by (rule, device) after a live-boot found a tag-scoped rule silently suppressing alerts on all but one of the devices it watched. Alerts publish into the unified notification feed under a new device.alert category, distinct from mymatasan's vision.alert so a subscriber can choose either or both. The device/profile/rule/alert/notification API surface is live (GET/POST /api/devices, /api/profiles, /api/rules, /api/alerts, /api/notifications); the SPA still only renders login/shell, with no device/rule UI yet. Also fixed a first-boot panic in the profile seeder (the generic repo's Delete returns an error, not a no-op, when it matches zero rows) and made the MQTT device authenticator distinguish a database-unreachable lookup failure from a genuinely wrong credential.
## 2026-07-14 — myiotsan 0.2.0, mymatasan 1.104.1, core 1.61.0 (acad78a)

### Added

- **myiotsan,core**: Added myiotsan, the suite's fourth app and on-prem IoT device hub ("the NVR, but for sensors"), as P0 scaffolding: a dedicated cmd/myiotsan entrypoint, config + dev TLS certs on port 3003, an app composition root that boots, authenticates (DB-backed local users via the shared appliance auth stack, with a new session-cookie login endpoint as its primary sign-in path instead of replaying HTTP Basic on every request), and serves an SPA shell. Its own three-role authorization catalog additionally makes device actuation admin-only, ahead of the actuation feature landing. The IoT domain itself (device inventory, telemetry ingest, rules, alerts) is deliberately not implemented yet and arrives in later phases (see docs/MYIOTSAN_PLAN.md).

### Changed

- **mymatasan**: Extracted mymatasan's local-auth stack (DB-backed users, Basic + session-cookie middleware, the failed-login lockout, and the three-role authorization matrix) into domain/shared so a second appliance app (myiotsan) runs the same security-critical code instead of a ~1,300-line fork. mymatasan's own types and routes are now thin aliases/bindings over the shared implementation; its authorization catalog (Policy()) stays app-owned. Also fixes a real bug affecting every app: an unmatched /api/* path previously returned 200 text/html to an unauthenticated caller instead of 404, because the app's auth middleware never saw a path that didn't match any of its routes.
## 2026-07-14 — mymatasan 1.104.0, myseliasan 1.28.0 (44268e8)

### Changed

- **myseliasan**: Gave myseliasan's per-node access grant (NodeAccessGrant) a third rung, CanOperate, so the control plane can map a fleet user onto a node's operator role instead of being forced into the node's admin or viewer role -- completing the fleet half of Tier 2 phase R (the mymatasan-side role model shipped in a prior change). The three flags are now an explicit escalation ladder (CanWrite implies CanOperate implies CanRead), enforced on save by services/node_access.go's normalizeAccess so a grant can never express an incoherent state like write-without-read, and re-normalised on read so a stale or hand-edited row cannot either. NodeAccess.Role() resolves the ladder to the role NAME sent over the tunnel (viewer/operator/admin), which the node evaluates against its own permission matrix -- the control plane never asserts a permission set, only an identity claim. The node_access_api.go audit trail now records the resolved (post-normalization) grant rather than the raw request body, since the two can differ. myseliasan's central RBAC Node Access matrix (RolesAccessPage) and its per-node access dropdown now expose all three levels.
- **mymatasan**: Replaced the Settings -> Users 'administrator' checkbox with a role picker, giving an admin a real UI for the three-role model (viewer/operator/admin) that previously required calling POST /api/settings/users directly with a roleId to assign anything other than admin or the isAdmin-derived default -- completing the frontend half of Tier 2 phase R. A new useRoles() hook fetches GET /api/settings/roles and a RoleSelect component renders the assignable roles on both the create-user form and each existing user's edit card; the user list badge now shows the assigned role's name instead of a binary Admin/non-admin badge. The update-user request now sends roleId alongside the legacy isAdmin bool. Live-verified against a running instance: creating a user with a roleId persists it, a viewer gets 200 on GET /api/cameras but 403 on /api/recording/segments and /api/settings/users, and an operator gets 200 on GET /api/recording/segments but 403 on DELETE and /api/system/reset.
## 2026-07-14 — mymatasan 1.103.0, core 1.60.0 (1a2fa14)

### Changed

- **mymatasan,core**: Replaced mymatasan's single admin/non-admin bool authorization with a three-role permission matrix (viewer, operator, admin), fixing two real security gaps: previously there was no read authorization at all (any signed-in user could read every camera, recording, and alert snapshot), and 'may watch cameras but may not delete recordings' was not expressible, the property that makes an NVR evidentiary rather than a camera viewer. mymatasan now reuses the shared accessrbac role/permission data model (domain/shared/services/access_rbac.go, whose path matcher was upgraded to segment-wise matching with a single-segment '*' wildcard, specificity-based row selection instead of raw string length, and a refusal to store a root-path '/' grant-everything row) with its own middleware (apis.NewRequireRolePermission), since mymatasan has no JWT to satisfy the shared session middleware. The authorization catalog is Go data (apps/mymatasan/services/rbac.go's Policy()): viewer watches live and sees that an alert fired with no footage access; operator adds reviewing/downloading recorded footage, acknowledging alerts, PTZ, and talk-back but can never delete or purge anything or reconfigure the system; admin (the shared superadmin builtin) bypasses the matrix. The parent-to-node control tunnel (apis/control_dispatch.go) now carries a role NAME rather than an admin/viewer bool, and the node resolves it against its own roles and evaluates its own matrix rather than trusting the parent's assertion, closing a projection gap where a fine-grained fleet role used to collapse into a raw admin/not-admin flag at every node.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.102.0, core 1.59.0 (a5c0678)

### Added

- **core,mymatasan**: Added a versioned, once-only schema migration mechanism (infra/db/bootstrap/migration.go) alongside the existing additive auto-migrator, plus a companion schema-drift detector (infra/db/bootstrap/drift.go) that reports what the additive path still cannot fix. The additive migrator could previously only ADD COLUMN: a rename silently stranded data in the old column, a drop left the column forever, and a type change was never even detected, so the row scanner could fail at runtime on a deployed appliance with no diagnostic trail. Migrations now run before auto-migrate in the boot pipeline (migrations -> auto-migrate -> drift check -> seeders), are checksummed so an already-applied migration cannot be silently edited, and a fresh database baselines them instead of replaying them (including through a factory reset, which now correctly threads its migration set through so a rebuilt database doesn't try to replay history against itself and fail to start). Apps opt in via the new apphost.Migrator interface; mymatasan implements it and currently registers zero migrations, since it has no pending structural changes today.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.101.0, core 1.58.0 (15e2fe7)

### Changed

- **mymatasan,core**: Tier 2, phase C: cut a per-app config seam so a config block no longer has to live in the shared infra/config.AppConfigModel just because mymatasan needs it. Six blocks that were mymatasan-only (camera, decoder, stream, vision, health, recording) — and the model types under them (StreamConfigModel, WebRTCConfigModel, MJPEGFallbackConfigModel, HealthConfigModel, RecordingConfigModel, VisionConfigModel, VisionTrainingConfigModel, VisionDetectorConfigModel) — moved to a new apps/mymatasan/config package. infra/apphost gained an optional AppConfigDecoder interface (DecodeAppConfig(raw, dataDir)) that an app implements to decode its own blocks straight out of the same raw config.json document the shared loader already parsed (AppConfigModel.Raw()); infra/apphost/run.go calls it once, after the shared config is loaded and normalized and before any route is registered, and aborts startup on a decode error. mymatasan also now normalizes its own data-relative paths (the recordings/snapshots root, the YOLO training dir) via Config.Normalize(dataDir) instead of infra/apphost/run.go doing it for it, so the generic application host no longer has hardcoded knowledge of a vision feature. What stayed in the shared model is deliberately narrower: security (encryption-at-rest, also used by myseliasan), pairing/nodeStream (the fleet), notification, and loginSecurity, plus every infra block. Along the way, fixed a real latent bug: infra/config.LoadAppConfiguration used to call json.Decode and discard the returned error, so a single JSON syntax error in config.json silently produced an all-zero config — the app booted on defaults, ignored everything the operator had configured, and said nothing about it. It now returns the error, and both the shared loader and the new apps/mymatasan/config.Load fail loudly on a malformed document. In apps/mymatasan/app, module gained a cfg *mmconfig.Config field populated by DecodeAppConfig, 7 of the 11 config_map.go mappers were retyped from *config.AppConfigModel to *mmconfig.Config, and the wiring struct gained an appCfg field plus a new validate() method that fails startup naming any wiring field left unset — added because appCfg was in fact left unset on the first cut of this change and panicked in registerRoutes at boot, a bug class that was worth guarding against for every field, not just this one.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.100.0 (c168567)

### Changed

- **mymatasan**: Decomposed mymatasan's app-composition root (Tier 2, phase D2): RegisterAppRoutes shrank from 792 to ~490 lines by moving per-subsystem wiring into seven new files (wire_security.go for the at-rest key/recovery-mode gate, wire_storage.go for the 16 repositories, wire_vision.go for the detector worker-script/model-pointer resolution, wire_fleet.go for the three node-dialed fleet channels, wire_routes.go for the middleware chain and API groups, wire_monitors.go for starting background workers and retention purges, wire_services.go for the wiring struct threaded between phases) plus config_map.go for the pure config-to-settings mappers. Pure refactor with no intended behavior change, but it converted two previously comment-enforced ordering hazards into compiler-enforced ones: the detector's resolved worker-script args are now a typed value (detectorModelPaths) passed explicitly instead of a silent deps.Config mutation, and the four MYMATASAN_*_FILE environment variables published to the Python YOLO worker now have one publication point (PublishToProcessEnv) instead of four bare os.Setenv calls. While decomposing, also found and fixed two latent bugs: the three fleet loops (enrollment/control/media channel managers) were unsupervised bare `go` calls, so a panic in any of them silently killed the whole process with no log line explaining why the node stopped enrolling, answering the parent, or relaying live video — all three are now safego.Supervise'd like the rest of the background workers.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.99.0 (22d5b05)

### Changed

- **mymatasan**: Replaced three hand-rolled, drift-prone constructions of recording.RecorderConfig (the startup recorder fan-out, the recording-settings save handler, and the detect-only stream resolver) with one shared services.RecorderConfigBuilder that every site calls. This removes the class of bug where a RecorderConfig field had to be remembered in three places or was silently dropped in the others — most notably ShredPasses, which was missing from the settings-save site and silently degraded secure shred to a plain unlink whenever an operator saved a recording setting. As a side effect, the at-rest storage codec (Settings -> Recording) is now read live on every recorder build instead of being captured once at process boot, so changing it now applies the next time a camera's recording config is saved instead of requiring a restart. The startup path also now logs (instead of silently swallowing) a failure to list recording configs, so a startup DB error no longer means recording silently never starts with nothing in the logs to explain why.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.98.0, core 1.57.0 (d13d497)

### Added

- **mymatasan,core**: Completes mymatasan Tier 1 (runtime metrics). Prometheus was already shipped and wired to /metrics, but nothing outside the shared API/coordination middleware ever recorded anything, so questions that matter on a customer box you cannot log into (is the detector keeping up, is ffmpeg thrashing, are segments failing to finalize, are notifications being dropped, why did recording stop overnight) had no answer at all. Adds a generic app-defined metrics registry to shared infra/telemetry (Labels + a Metrics interface: Describe/Inc/Add/Set/Observe, counters/gauges/millisecond histograms with arbitrary label sets, backed by the existing Prometheus recorder and exposed to every app as apphost.Dependencies.Metrics, never nil) with a hard cardinality cap (500 series per metric name) whose truncation is surfaced as a <name>_series_truncated gauge rather than silently dropping series. mymatasan now emits mymatasan_inference_duration_ms, mymatasan_frames_total, mymatasan_alerts_total, mymatasan_camera_online, mymatasan_cameras_offline, mymatasan_disk_used_percent, mymatasan_recording_paused, and mymatasan_disk_mitigation_total from the vision monitor and the camera/machine health monitors; shared infra/recording emits kopiv2_recording_ffmpeg_restarts_total and kopiv2_recording_segment_finalize_total (by outcome, including a separately-counted quarantined outcome); shared infra/notification emits kopiv2_notification_delivery_total (by channel and outcome) so an at-most-once delivery drop is no longer only a log line. Also wraps the camera-health monitor's per-camera probe goroutines in safego.Go, since as bare `go` calls they were child goroutines a panic in them would not have been caught by the safego.Supervise wrapping the monitor's run loop.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.97.2, core 1.56.2 (d57968d)

### Fixed

- **mymatasan,core**: Tier 1 resilience hardening: a new shared infra/safego package gives background goroutines panic recovery (safego.Go, one-shot) and recover-and-restart-with-backoff supervision (safego.Supervise, long-lived loops). Before this, the repo had exactly one recover() anywhere, so a single malformed AI detection in the per-camera vision sampler could panic and kill the entire NVR process (recording, API, everything); the vision monitor, camera/machine health monitors, object-metadata recorder, RTSP recorder's ffmpeg/segment-watch/purge goroutines, the discovery responder, and detector warmup are now wired through safego, and mymatasan's app logger is set as safego's panic sink at startup. In apps/mymatasan/app/app.go, the three near-identical copy-pasted retention purge goroutines (segments+observations, notifications, alerts) were collapsed into one supervised periodic() helper, and previously-swallowed purge errors are now logged. Separately, AI rule cooldown now survives a restart: cooldown state was purely in-process and started empty on every boot, so a busy scene re-fired the instant minFrames was met after any restart, and a crash-restart loop could turn a rule's cooldown into an alert storm, a notification storm, and an ffmpeg clip-extraction storm. A rule's already-persisted LastTriggeredAt (loaded from the DB but never previously read back into the cooldown check, and never written to) now seeds the in-process cooldown map on first sight of the rule (infra/vision/cooldown.go), and the vision monitor now writes the trigger time back via a new IVisionService.MarkRuleTriggered after each sample. A dropped CreateAlert call is now logged instead of silently discarded.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.97.1, core 1.56.1 (405d5af)

### Fixed

- **mymatasan,core**: Tier 0 correctness hardening for mymatasan: RTSP segment finalization is now crash-safe (remux writes to a .mp4.part and is only renamed into place after encryption succeeds, so a bare .mp4 on disk is always complete and encrypted, and a .ts sibling always means the segment was never finalized) with a bounded-retry quarantine directory for segments that repeatedly fail to remux; deleting a camera now cascades through a registered cleanup chain (stop recorder/detect-only stream, purge footage, purge object-metadata observations, delete detection rules, purge alert events, delete recording config) and aborts if any step fails, instead of leaving an orphaned recorder and rules running forever; footage retention purge no longer freezes a camera's existing recordings when recording is toggled off, and now drains in batches so a backlog can actually catch up instead of capping at one page per sweep; the recording config API now correctly threads the shredPasses setting into every recorder rebuild it triggers; and the highest-write alert_event table gained (camera_id, created_at) and (created_at) indexes, enabled by a schema-tag change letting one column join multiple index groups.
## 2026-07-13 — mymatasan 1.97.0, myseliasan 1.27.0, core 1.56.0 (a485631)

### Changed

- **shared,mymatasan,myseliasan**: Added a new shared @shared/CameraHero component (breadcrumb trail + status-tinted camera tile + name/description + health/stream status chips, tokenized CSS via color-mix) and made it THE standardized camera-page header across the suite: mymatasan's camera detail page, which previously had no header at all above its tab bar, now renders it with a 'Cameras > <camera>' breadcrumb wired through the side-nav's own root selector so the rail highlight follows; myseliasan's Nodes -> Camera page now renders it in place of its old flat title row (which used to show the camera's ONVIF/host URL under the name - that URL moved out of the header but is still shown as ONVIF URI/Host chips in the Live tab's info grid), with a 'Nodes > <node> > <camera>' breadcrumb. Also in this batch: (1) the shared dashboard header (.dashboard-head, duplicated in both apps' CSS) changed from a wrapping flex row to a column - title/subtitle on their own line, then the Today/7 Days/30 Days range + Refresh toolbar right-aligned on the line below - fixing a bug where the toolbar rendered inline in one app and wrapped to the left edge in the other because the old row layout was column-width-dependent; (2) myseliasan's login/forced-password-change/pending-clearance screens were missing '.login-screen { position: relative }' and the '.login-lang-switch { position: absolute }' rule mymatasan already has, so the top-corner language switcher was an in-flow flex child that pushed the centered login card off-center - fixed to match mymatasan; (3) myseliasan's side-nav gained the pin/auto-hide toggle already shipped in mymatasan (collapses the rail to a 68px hover-expanding icon strip, persisted to localStorage as myseliasan_nav_pinned), guarded at min-width:1081px since myseliasan's rail stacks at a different breakpoint (<=1080px) than mymatasan's (<=860px); (4) added shared i18n keys common.breadcrumb and nav.pin/nav.autohide (all 4 locales); the nav.pin/nav.autohide additions back myseliasan's new toggle, which has no app-level override, and their wording deliberately matches mymatasan's existing app-dictionary strings so the two rails show identical tooltips.
## 2026-07-13 — myseliasan 1.26.0 (2353e2b)

### Added

- **myseliasan**: MySeliaSan can now discover and add cameras to an adopted node, from the control plane. A new 'Cameras' tab in the node Manage dialog (Adopted Nodes -> Manage) hosts mymatasan's real Discover page: a network scan (ONVIF WS-Discovery, SSDP/UPnP, mDNS, SADP, or a subnet port scan), a manual add-by-address probe, the discovered-device list split into new vs already-added, and the credential dialog that verifies the camera login before saving. The scan runs ON the node and the subnet box is prefilled from the node's own interfaces - not the operator's - because camera discovery is multicast and does not route off the LAN, so a scan issued from the control plane's own network would find nothing. No backend work was needed: the node command tunnel is a generic pass-through, so POST /api/nodes/{id}/proxy/api/onvif/{discover,scan,probe} and /api/cameras/discovered already reached the node, are already admin-only (the node rejects a non-GET from a viewer), and are already recorded in the audit log as node.command. A scan against an offline node now reports that the node did not respond, instead of silently reading as 'no cameras found'.
## 2026-07-13 — myidsan 1.12.1, mymatasan 1.96.1, myseliasan 1.25.1, core 1.55.0 (e029430)

### Added

- **myseliasan,core**: MySeliaSan is now a downloadable, installable product on the r450k download page, packaged to full parity with MyMataSan: portable archives (linux/windows/darwin x amd64/arm64), .deb and .rpm packages that install to /opt/myseliasan with a systemd unit and a dedicated service user, a Windows Inno Setup installer that registers a service and generates a one-time admin password, and multi-arch GHCR Docker images. It is pure Go - no ffmpeg, no Python, no model weights - so the payload is just the binary, the web UI and a default config. myseliasan releases under its OWN tag namespace (myseliasan-v<ver>) and is published with --latest=false, so it is versioned and released independently of mymatasan (a bump to either ships that product alone) and never becomes the repo's 'latest' release, which mymatasan's in-app updater reads. GoReleaser OSS cannot derive a semver from a prefixed tag (monorepo/tag_prefix is a Pro feature), so the release workflow hands it a plain version via GORELEASER_CURRENT_TAG, disables its GitHub-release step, and publishes with 'gh release create' itself. The r450k download page now resolves the newest release per product from separate tags (it can no longer use /releases/latest, which only ever returns mymatasan's) and renders a download block per product. Also fixed a packaging blocker: myseliasan's RegisterWebRoutes resolved static/index.html from the CWD-relative BaseDir() rather than deps.HomeDir, so a packaged install (binary and static/ side by side, service working directory elsewhere) would have 404'd on / and /index.html. The self-signed certificate the app generates on first boot is now issued for the running app instead of always claiming CommonName=mymatasan.

### Changed

- **shared,mymatasan,myseliasan,myidsan**: The brand mark and the login screen are now standardized across every app. The shield/eye/checkmark mark moved into the shared UI module as @shared/BrandLogo (with a matching brand.css), so the three hand-copied SVGs can no longer drift; each app supplies only its wordmark and a --brand-mark hue token, deliberately set outside the theme blocks so the brand keeps its identity in light and dark. The geometry and saturation are identical and only the hue moves - mymatasan green, myseliasan steel, myidsan violet - so the apps read as one family while staying tellable apart in a browser tab strip. The password input with its in-field reveal toggle moved to @shared/PasswordField on the same basis. MySeliaSan gained a real favicon (its previous .ico was an unrelated image) and its login, forced-password-change and pending-clearance screens now wear mymatasan's login-panel chrome. MyIDSan had no favicon and no asset pipeline at all; it now has both, and its server-rendered federated sign-in page - the page MySeliaSan hands the browser to during SSO - wears the same card, so the whole federated flow looks like one product. A failed login on that page now re-renders the branded card with the error inline and the username preserved, instead of dumping unstyled text.

### Fixed

- **myidsan**: The federated sign-in page no longer offers Google/GitHub buttons for providers that have no credentials configured. socialButtonsHTML gated the buttons on the provider config block being non-nil, but the stock config.json ships google and github objects with empty client_id/client_secret - a present-but-blank provider is still non-nil, so both buttons rendered and sent the browser to accounts.google.com with an empty client_id. That is an OAuth error on the public internet, and a dead end on an air-gapped intranet, which is where MySeliaSan (whose SSO hop lands on this page) is deployed. A provider is now considered configured only when both client_id and client_secret are non-empty. Covered by tests that assert the buttons disappear for blank credentials and that the page references no external host at all.
- **mymatasan,myseliasan**: The 4953x -> 3953x fleet pairing port move never reached the config that actually ships. deploy/dist/config.json is the default config bundled into MyMataSan's archives, .deb/.rpm, Docker image and Windows installer, and it was left on mtlsPort 49532 with controlPort/mediaPort unset (so they fell back to the Go constants 49533/49534). The dev configs under apps/ were moved to 39532/39533/39534, but a packaged install - the only kind an end user gets - kept the very Windows port conflict the move was made to avoid. deploy/dist/config.json now states mtlsPort 39532, controlPort 39533 and mediaPort 39534 explicitly, and MySeliaSan's new shipped config matches, so a packaged parent and its packaged nodes agree out of the box.

### Security

- **mymatasan**: The in-app self-updater can no longer install a different product over MyMataSan. selectAssets matched a release asset on the '_<os>_<arch>' suffix and file extension alone, with no product prefix. Now that MySeliaSan releases out of the same repository, its archives carry an identical suffix and extension (myseliasan_1.9.0_windows_amd64.zip), so had a myseliasan release ever been the repository's 'latest', every deployed node would have matched that asset, downloaded it, verified it against the shared checksums.txt, and overwritten itself with the control plane. Asset selection now requires the 'mymatasan_' product prefix, so an asset can only ever be this product's archive. myseliasan releases are additionally published under their own tag namespace with --latest=false, so they never become 'latest' in the first place - this prefix check is the second, independent guard.
- **myseliasan**: MySeliaSan no longer falls back to a guessable default superadmin credential. EnsureStockSuperadmin silently substituted the literal password 'admin' whenever the configured localAuth.password was empty, which meant a shipped package with a blank config password would have stood up an admin/admin superadmin on the box that controls the entire camera fleet. The password is now resolved as LOCAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD (ops override), then the config value, and otherwise a strong 16-character per-install password generated from crypto/rand - which is printed to the log and saved to INITIAL_ADMIN_LOGIN.txt in the data dir, exactly as mymatasan already does. A generated password is deliberately not re-applied on subsequent boots, so a restart cannot rotate the credential out from under an operator who just read it out of that file. Adds the lock-out recovery path mymatasan already had: a RESET_ADMIN marker file in the data dir (dropped by the Windows installer's 'reset the admin login' option, or by hand) force-resets the superadmin password on next start, re-enabling the account and re-flagging it must-change; the marker is deleted before the reset runs so it can never fire twice. The login screen also asks the server whether federated sign-in is configured (GET /api/auth/config) and hides the 'Continue with myidsan' button when sso.providerBaseUrl is empty, so a standalone install does not present a primary button that cannot work.
## 2026-07-13 — myseliasan 1.22.0 (92f2b85)

### Added

- **myseliasan**: myseliasan's control plane was a passive relay that only showed a node's status when someone opened the Nodes page; it now proactively alerts on fleet health. The heartbeat reconciler (services/node_registry.go) edge-triggers a notification in the unified feed the instant a node goes online→lost (once, not repeated while it stays lost), when a lost node recovers, and when a node's certificate is within its renewal warn window of expiring or has already expired (deduped per distinct expiry, so a renewal re-arms the warning). The warn window defaults to 7 days and is wired from the existing pairing.renewBeforeHours config (previously only consulted by the node side for its own renewal, so this makes a config key that already existed do double duty rather than adding a new one). A new GET /api/nodes/fleet-status endpoint returns a fleet-wide rollup (total/online/lost/selfDropped/unknown/certsExpiring/certsExpired/certWarnDays), and the Dashboard gained a 'Certs expiring' KPI card fed by it. Separately, ingestNodeEvent no longer silently drops node-pushed event kinds it doesn't recognize (health/disk-full/alert/system/etc.) — it now surfaces them in the notification feed with a best-effort category/severity guess instead of discarding them.
- **myseliasan**: myseliasan gains an immutable audit log of sensitive control-plane actions. A new append-only audit_log table (entities/audit_log.go) and IAuditService (services/audit.go) record who did what, to what, with what outcome, whenever an operator adopts/releases a node (node.adopt/node.release, the latter noting certificate revocation), rotates the fleet key (fleet.key_rotate — the key value itself is never recorded), changes a user's role/enabled state or elevates them to superadmin (rbac.set_role/rbac.set_disabled/rbac.elevate, with before->after role captured), grants or revokes per-node access (node_access.set/node_access.revoke), or sends a mutating command through the reverse node tunnel (node.command, covering the single choke point through which remote wipe/factory-reset/settings-writes reach a node; read-only GET/HEAD tunneled traffic is not audited). A node reporting its own unpairing (node.self_dropped) is attributed to the node rather than an operator. Recording is best-effort and never blocks or fails the action being audited. The trail is readable only via a new superadmin-gated GET /api/audit endpoint (apis/audit.go), with a matching Audit Log page in the SPA (filter by action/target, under Administration) added to the side-nav, App shell, and i18n (en/ms/zh/ar).
- **myseliasan**: myseliasan now implements apphost's ReadinessReporter interface, surfacing fleet-listener health as advisory fields on GET /api/ready: controlChannel (up/down, whether the parent<->node control-channel listener's serve loop is active), connectedNodes (count of nodes currently holding a live control connection), and mediaRelay (up/down, whether the node-camera media relay listener is active). ControlServer gained IsListening() (atomic bool set around its serve loop) and ConnectedCount(); the app module is now stateful, holding the *ControlServer and an atomic media-listening flag set during RegisterAppRoutes, with a new ReadinessStatus method feeding them into the shared /ready handler. This closes the gap where a dead control-channel or media listener still reported the process fully ready.

### Changed

- **myseliasan**: Three control-plane scale/resilience fixes for larger or partially-unreachable fleets. Heartbeat (node_registry.go) previously mTLS-probed nodes without a live control connection one at a time, so a handful of unreachable nodes could blow an entire sweep past the heartbeat interval; probing now runs through a bounded worker pool (heartbeatProbeConcurrency = 16) under a per-sweep budget (heartbeatSweepBudget = 30s) via new probeNodesConcurrently, while DB reconciliation/writes stay serial for the single-writer sqlite store. NodeRegistry.List previously called Get(...,1000,0,...) once, silently truncating the fleet at 1000 adopted nodes and cascading into the operator Nodes list, Scan adoption dedup, and Heartbeat itself; it now pages through the table in nodeListPageSize (500) chunks until exhausted. control_server.go now fails fast when a node's control channel drops mid-command: pending requests are tracked with their owning nodeID (pendingReq), and on disconnect failPending(nodeID) delivers a nil frame to every waiter bound to that node so SendRequest returns the new ErrNodeDisconnected immediately instead of hanging to controlRequestTimeout (30s); node_proxy.go maps it to the same 404 "node is not connected" as ErrNodeOffline. Idempotency keys for tunneled writes were not implemented in this change (would need node-side dedup support in mymatasan), so a non-idempotent write in flight at disconnect time has an unknown outcome and is not automatically retried.
- **myseliasan**: Restyled the superadmin Audit Log page to match the app's standard admin-page layout (settings-panel + header + settings-hint, the same shape as the Users and Roles pages), replacing the bespoke action/target-type <select> filter controls with the shared DataTable's native per-column filtering and sorting. Removed the now-dead .filter-select and .audit-tab.* CSS and 4 i18n keys (audit.allActions/allTargets/filterAction/filterTarget) that backed the old controls.

### Security

- **myseliasan**: The fleet mTLS CA private key, the parent leaf private key, and the fleet PSK previously sat in plaintext in the myseliasan control-plane database (ControlSetting rows) — anyone able to read that database could impersonate the fleet CA or the shared secret and take over every adopted node. These three values are now AES-256-GCM encrypted at rest (reusing the existing infra/atrest module, the same one mymatasan uses for recordings/snapshots/training images) whenever encryption at rest is enabled. New apps/myseliasan/services/secret_store.go adds encodeSecret/decodeSecret helpers (base64(atrest ciphertext) in the TEXT settings column); fleet_ca.go and node_registry.go route the CA/parent private keys and the fleet PSK through them. app.go adds openFleetSecretCipher, which resolves the cipher from the same shared `security` config block mymatasan already documents (encryptAtRest, keyPath, keyProtector, passphrase/passphraseFile/passphraseEnv, recoveryPath) before the node registry is built. Public certificates and the revocation list are unaffected and remain plaintext.
## 2026-07-12 — myseliasan 1.16.0 (03687e0)

### Security

- **myseliasan**: Extends the OWASP-ZAP-driven security-headers hardening already shipped for mymatasan to myseliasan: config.json/config.dev.json now set the same tested securityHeaders.contentSecurityPolicy on the shared infra/apphost security-headers middleware, closing a ZAP 'CSP header not set' Medium finding. The front-end's Quicksand font is now self-hosted (assets/fonts.css + woff2, copied from mymatasan) instead of loaded from Google Fonts, removing the app's only cross-origin resource and closing a ZAP 'Sub-Resource-Integrity missing' Medium finding; a broken %PUBLIC_URL%/favicon.ico template token was also fixed to /favicon.ico. tools/zaproxy/ dev tooling was extended to scan myseliasan (JSON-login auth, cookie session, OpenAPI import, CSRF double-submit HttpSender script) — that tooling is developer-only, like tools/tgbridge, with no shipped-app doc/changelog coverage of its own.
## 2026-07-12 — mymatasan 1.94.0 (26b8f4e)

### Changed

- **mymatasan**: The YOLO worker's stock+custom detection merge (_merge in ai/yolo_worker.py) now gives a taught (custom) model priority over the stock model where it fires: custom detections are kept first, then any stock detection whose box overlaps a kept custom detection (IoU > 0.55) is dropped regardless of label, so e.g. a stock "dog" and custom "cat" on the same animal collapse to one "cat" detection instead of both. Stock detections outside a custom detection's footprint are untouched, so activating a skill still adds its classes elsewhere in the frame rather than blinding stock detection. Because _merge output feeds the whole detection pipeline (not just the Object Search metadata siphon), this also changes AI alert-rule evaluation: a rule targeting a stock class will no longer fire on an object the active taught model has relabeled to a different class.
## 2026-07-12 — mymatasan 1.93.1, myseliasan 1.15.0, core 1.53.3 (e4ac91b)

### Added

- **mymatasan,myseliasan**: myseliasan now embeds each managed node's real mymatasan camera pages (Live View with relay video/PTZ/audio, Detection, Recordings, Settings) directly inside the control plane, driven entirely over the existing node-dialed control and media channels so the browser never talks to the node directly. The camera tabs reuse mymatasan's actual React components (copied into components/nodecam/, routed via a proxy apiBase + CSRF fetch shim) and mymatasan's actual stylesheet (imported via a new @mymatasan webpack alias, injected once and CSS-scoped under .nodecam-embed) so the embedded pages track mymatasan's design automatically on future builds. New cross-node Live Views wall and a node dashboard mirroring mymatasan's own analytics dashboard were added, plus per-section version banners so an older node shows a clear notice instead of a silent blank panel. Because recorded clips can be larger than the control channel's 16 MiB per-message cap and encrypted/HEVC clips aren't seekable end-to-end, mymatasan's recording download endpoint now serves HTTP Range requests (materializing a seekable decrypted/transcoded temp copy on first touch), the node's tunnel dispatcher forwards all response headers (not just Content-Type), and myseliasan exposes a new GET /api/nodes/{id}/recording-stream/{segId} endpoint that chunks each browser Range request under the tunnel's message cap and returns 206 Partial Content, enabling seekable recording playback through the embedded Recordings tab. Also fixed a dashboard request-storm caused by unstable effect dependencies and stopped index.html from being browser-cached (it references content-hashed bundle filenames, so a stale cached copy could keep a browser on an old bundle after a rebuild).
- **mymatasan,myseliasan**: myseliasan's fleet control plane gains a consolidated Notifications page (badged nav item under a new System group) that lists its own events plus every event a managed node pushes up its control channel, with unread/all + per-node source filters, infinite scroll, live SSE updates, and an Acknowledge action that marks the notification read (new POST /api/notifications/{id}/read) and propagates AI-detection acknowledgements back to the source node alert over the proxy. The Nodes page gains a pop-up Adopt dialog (from a discovered node or a manual-entry button, for nodes outside multicast range) and a tabbed node Settings modal (Details/Camera Health/Users/Backup & Recovery/Version & Health) styled like mymatasan's own Settings page, all tunneled over the node proxy; Details edits the node's operator-facing name/description/icon via a new PUT /api/nodes/{id}, and Version & Health mirrors mymatasan's software version, update check/apply, and live service health/readiness plus host CPU/mem/disk. Each adopted node also gets a Wipe action that remotely factory-resets it (mirroring mymatasan's own secure wipe) behind an allowReset gate check and an auto-proceeding confirmation countdown. The cross-node Live Views wall was rebuilt for full parity with mymatasan's own Live Views: grid layout picker, pagination, fullscreen, drag-to-reorder tiles, per-tile maximize and PTZ, and an in-wall cross-node Add-camera picker. Per-node device access levels were renamed viewer/superadmin to viewer/admin throughout the RBAC UI and API docs to avoid confusion with RBAC's own superadmin role; Users/Roles admin tables gained pagination, and Roles gained Rename + Refresh. Side-nav: Live Views moved above the Nodes tree, both tree levels now expand/collapse on double-click, and the nav list area scrolls independently of the fixed header/footer chrome. Underlying this, the shared apphost runtime (infra/apphost/run.go) now mirrors the root /ready readiness probe under /api/ready on every app, since a reverse control tunnel like myseliasan's only dispatches against the /api subrouter and could not reach the root path — this is what powers the Version & Health tab's readiness pill and requires managed mymatasan nodes to be rebuilt to expose it.
- **mymatasan,myseliasan**: mymatasan's Recording tab gains a per-camera 'Purge now' action that deletes ALL recorded footage and AI-event snapshots for that camera immediately, ignoring the configured retention period — for when an operator needs a camera's history gone right away rather than waiting on the retention sweep. It is backed by a new POST /api/recording/purge-camera endpoint (recordingService.PurgeAllForCamera for footage, deleting rows and securely removing files oldest-first, plus visionService.PurgeAlertsForCamera for the camera's alert rows and deduped snapshot files, injected as a new dependency into the recording API), and gated behind a 5-second cancellable countdown confirmation mirroring the factory-reset wipe; only the Recording tab's own segment list refreshes afterward, not the whole page. myseliasan's embedded node camera Recording tab (the mymatasan Recordings component reused over the control tunnel) gets the identical action, tunneled to the node's endpoint over the existing node proxy.
- **myseliasan**: myseliasan's landing Dashboard tab, previously minimal, is rebuilt into a fleet-wide analytics view mirroring mymatasan's own camera dashboard (same @shared/charts KPI/timeseries layout), but aggregated by adopted NODE instead of camera. It is backed by a new GET /api/notifications/stats endpoint that aggregates myseliasan's own notifications table (both its own control-plane events and every node-pushed event already ingested into that table) over a selectable Today/7-day/30-day window, bucketed by hour or day and aligned to the viewer's local timezone offset, with a per-node activity breakdown (Stats.BySource, keyed 'node:<id>'). No per-node fan-out request is needed since the control plane already stores every event locally.
- **myseliasan**: Added a fleet-wide Teach nav item to myseliasan. Because teaching (capture -> train -> activate) runs entirely on the node itself, the flow is 'Where-first': the operator picks a target camera through a Node-to-Camera tree dropdown, and the real mymatasan Teach wizard (its source copied under components/nodecam/teach.js, matching the existing embedded-node-page pattern) then runs against that camera's node over the control tunnel, pre-scoped to the chosen camera. A second 'Share' tab lets an operator push a trained skill across the fleet in one action: export a skill from a source node as an encrypted .mmskill package over the proxy, then import it into any number of selected target nodes; because both the export payload and each import travel through the control tunnel's frame/body size caps, the push defaults to model-only (no training images).

### Changed

- **shared,mymatasan,myseliasan**: Added a standardized Tabs component to the shared frontend module (frontend/shared/src/Tabs.js + styles/tabs.css) and migrated every tab bar across mymatasan (Cameras, Objects, Settings, Teach) and myseliasan (Objects, Teach, node Settings dialog, node manager) onto it, replacing hand-rolled per-page tab markup that had drifted to inconsistent bar-to-content gaps (18px/16px/12px across different pages) and button heights. The shared component now owns one fixed 16px gap and a uniform 38px tab height with an accent-underline active state everywhere, and adds a new --ui-accent-soft design token (mapped in every app's theme, including myidsan) for the active-tab tint. Dead per-page tab CSS (.settings-tabs, .camera-detail-tabs, etc.) was removed from mymatasan's and myseliasan's stylesheets now that the shared component supplies it.
